# The decline index

**This file is the decline index: the single ground-truth for *where* every recorded Bezel decline, deferral, non-goal, and retirement lives.** Bezel's declined-with-reasons content is recorded in **five** homes, not one — (1) the rationale's explicit-rejection register (§17), (2) its deferred register (§18), (3) this file's own declined register (part 2 below, plus the gap review it points at), (4) per-section decision records scattered through the specification and the rationale — where most of the substantive declines actually live — and (5) decline records inside graduated proposals. The topology is stated in [proposal 0106 §0](0106-language-lessons-survey.md); its research run paid the five-register walk repeatedly, once per verdict. The project's reopening discipline — *revisiting an entry requires new evidence against the stated reasons, not re-asking* — depends on being able to find the stated reasons. This index is the fix, and it is an **index, not a move**: no record is relocated and no reason is restated here. **A row points; the cited record is always the authority.** Where a row's summary and its authoritative record disagree, the record wins and the row is a defect.

**Verdict classes** (closed set — every row carries exactly one):

| Class | Means | Archetype |
|---|---|---|
| `rejected` | Decided against on principle. Reversible only against the stated reasons; many rows still carry a bar, which is what it would take, not a promise. A row that has *already* been reversed stays here rather than moving — the entry survives as the precedent that a rejection can move (rationale §17.6's shape). | rationale §17 |
| `deferred` | Not in this revision; the design is open. Reopenable by the stated condition. | rationale §18 |
| `declined-at-proposal` | Declined in a proposals-folder record — this file's D-numbers, the gap review, or a decline record inside a graduated proposal. | `DECLINED.md`, proposal records |
| `non-goal` | A deliberate absence of a whole area, recorded normatively in the specification. | spec §2.8.5 |
| `retired` | Shipped, then removed. The record is the retirement's reasoning. | the `event` member kind (spec §24) |
| `recommended — awaiting owner ruling` | Not yet a verdict: a draft proposal recommends recording a decline. No reader may cite one of these as settled. | a draft proposal's recommended decline |

The archetype column names where the class *usually* lives, not the only place it may live: a rejection recorded in a per-section spec decision record is still `rejected`, and a deferral recorded inside a graduated proposal is still `deferred`.

**Reading the table.** Rows are grouped by verdict class, alphabetical by topic within a group. **Authoritative record** is a file plus a section: `rationale` is `docs/Bezel_Design_Rationale.md`, `spec` is `docs/Bezel_Language_Spec.md`, `stdlib` is `docs/Bezel_Standard_Library.md`, `changelog` is `docs/Bezel_Changelog.md`; proposal citations link to the file in this folder. Section numbers are as of **v0.269**; cite them onward with the revision, per `CLAUDE.md`. **Reopening bar** quotes or tightly paraphrases the condition the record states, or reads `—(none stated)` when the record states none — which means *no path is recorded*, not *no path exists*.

**Keeping it true.** Every landing that records a new decline, deferral, non-goal, or retirement — or graduates or declines a proposal — adds or updates its row here in the same commit ([`spec-change-process.md` §9](spec-change-process.md)). A row without a resolvable citation is a defect in this file, not in the record.

---

## The index

### `rejected`

| Topic | Verdict class | Authoritative record | Reopening bar |
|---|---|---|---|
| A nested type of a generic type seeing its enclosing type's type parameters, one nested type per enclosing instantiation (`Outer<int>.Inner` distinct from `Outer<string>.Inner` — C#'s nested types, Java's inner classes) | `rejected` | spec §11.6, §2.8.3 rule 3, Appendix C.1 (v0.314); rationale §9.10; changelog v0.314 ([#5366](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5366)) | Identity has no component for the enclosing arguments and A.5 no spelling for `Outer<int>.Inner` in a type; admitting the capture is a design change across identity, the type grammar and every per-instantiation rule, for no recorded need. A nested type declares its own parameter instead (`Node<T>`). Reopening bar: a recorded need the own-parameter form cannot meet. |
| A bare enclosing segment exempt from arity selection, so `Tree.Node<int>` names the nested type of a generic-only `Tree<T>` | `rejected` | spec §11.6, §2.2 *Collisions*, §14.1 (v0.316); rationale §9.10 (v0.316 paragraph); changelog v0.316 ([#5386](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5386)) | The segment's tokens would not fix its arity (v0.303's raw-reference decline, v0.311's #5164 decline), and an added import of a non-generic `Tree` declaring its own `Node<U>` would silently re-resolve the chain, against §2.7. A library wanting callers to name the type declares it top-level. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Rendering an enclosing generic type in a nested type's `FullName` without its arity (`"M.Tree.Node<int>"`) or with a counted marker (``"M.Tree`1.Node<int>"``) | `rejected` | stdlib §2.13 rule 1 (v0.316); rationale §9.10 (v0.316 paragraph); changelog v0.316 ([#5386](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5386)) | Without the arity, the nested types of the arity twins `Outer` and `Outer<T>`, two types under spec §2.8.3 rule 3, render one FullName; a counted marker is the arity marker rule 3 declines. One `_` per parameter (`"M.Tree<_>.Node<int>"`) renders it. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Rejecting an unbound temporary or a scope-bound local passed to a plain parameter because the callee's body stores the parameter (a callee-body retention summary at the call) | `rejected` | spec §28.3 (*Borrow lifetime rules*, the field-store bullet's residual; *Unbound owned temporaries*), K8.4 (v0.314); rationale §5.24 (v0.314 addendum); changelog v0.314 ([#5360](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5360)) | The caller's verdict would depend on another declaration's body, which no per-body fact carries (proposal 0105 §1), and would change when the callee became virtual, an interface member, or imported. It generalizes the extension §15.4 declines for `Add`/`Insert`. A temporary is rejected where a declaration announces the retention; `scoped` is that announcement for a back-reference. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Keeping an unbound temporary alive past its full-expression boundary while a callee may retain it (retention-conditional lifetime extension) | `rejected` | spec K8.1, K8.4, §28.3 (*Unbound owned temporaries*, v0.314); rationale §5.24 (v0.314 addendum); changelog v0.314 ([#5360](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5360)) | The disposal point would depend on another body, the extended lifetime is invisible at the call, and the stored reference still dangles once the extension ends. Bezel has no general lifetime-extension mechanism (K8.4; rationale §5.24's two recorded stretches are construct-bounded). Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Admitting a bare leading or trailing point (`".5"`, `"5."`) in `decimal.Parse`/`TryParse`/`ParseOrNull` and `Convert.ToDecimal`/`TryToDecimal` | `rejected` | stdlib §9.3 (v0.314); rationale §14.9; changelog v0.314 ([#5368](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5368)) | The `decimal` literal (spec Appendix A `FloatLiteral`) and binary-float `Parse` (§9.2) both need digits on both sides of a point, and no `decimal` rendering writes a bare one; one shape for every numeric `Parse`, and a later admission would be a widening. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Truncating to its width an integer component narrower than 64 bits whose value read does not fit, or raising `OverflowException` from the synthesized `Deserialize`; admitting to JSON `ReadInt`/`ReadUInt` a number with a fraction or an exponent (`5.0`, `1e3`) | `rejected` | spec §9.8 (v0.314); stdlib §2.15 (v0.314); rationale §5.19; changelog v0.314 ([#5369](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5369)) | Truncation decodes a different, plausible value with no signal; the value is the input's, so the fault is the input's `SerializationException`, as for a `float` component (#5200). `WriteInt`/`WriteUInt` never write a fraction or an exponent, and admitting one later is a widening. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Admitting `strtod`'s wider input forms in binary-float `Parse`/`TryParse`/`ParseOrNull` and `Convert.ToDouble`/`TryToDouble`: hexadecimal significands (`"0x1p3"`) and a bare leading or trailing point (`".5"`, `"5."`) | `rejected` | stdlib §9.2 (v0.314); rationale §14.9; changelog v0.314 ([#5199](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5199)) | No literal (spec Appendix A `FloatLiteral`), rendering (stdlib §2.18) or JSON number uses them; the integer and `decimal` grammars (§9.1, §9.3) are stated without a C library; and the narrow grammar is the compatible start, a later admission being a widening. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| JSON `ReadFloat` yielding `±Infinity` for a finite JSON number whose `double` rounding is not finite, or a `float` component yielding `±Infinity` from a finite number beyond `float`'s range | `rejected` | stdlib §2.15 (v0.314); spec §9.8 (v0.314); rationale §14.9; changelog v0.314 ([#5200](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5200)) | A document would decode to a non-finite value `WriteFloat` refuses to write, and one digit string would fault in `double.Parse` (§9.2) and decode silently to an infinity. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Reading a JSON number into a `float` component by one rounding of its text to `float`, rather than `float(x)` over `ReadFloat`'s `double` | `rejected` | spec §9.8 (v0.314); rationale §14.9; changelog v0.314 ([#5200](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5200)) | The deserializer vocabulary has one float read (stdlib §2.14); a single rounding needs a second `IDeserializer` member every format implementation must supply, or a JSON-private path synthesis cannot name. Reopening bar: a `float` read added to `IDeserializer` for another reason. |
| Rendering a non-finite `double`/`float` in the C library's spellings (`nan`, `inf`, `-inf`), or with a specifier's digits or `%` suffix; accepting in binary-float `Parse` any spelling of a non-finite value besides exactly `NaN`, `Infinity` and `-Infinity` (`infinity`, `nan(…)`, another case, `+Infinity`) | `rejected` | stdlib §2.18, §9.2 (v0.316); rationale §14.9; changelog v0.316 ([#4890](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4890)) | The three tokens are the constants' names and spec §4.3's spelling; a non-finite value has no digits to lay out; the round trip needs only the rendered spellings, and `strtod`'s set would tie the contract to the C library. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Serializing only a derived class's own components; rejecting synthesis, in either direction or for `Deserialize` only, over a base class that declares components; nesting a base's components as a sub-object on the wire | `rejected` | spec §9.8 (v0.316); rationale §5.22; changelog v0.316 ([#2364](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/2364)) | An own-components form does not round-trip the instance; a rejection strands the concrete-base `polymorphic` shape, and a `Deserialize`-only rejection makes eligibility direction-dependent; nesting gives one instance two wire objects. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Synthesizing a derived class's serialized form over a base that declares components but neither serialization interface itself, or that hand-writes `Serialize` or `Deserialize` | `rejected` | spec §9.8 (v0.316); rationale §5.22; changelog v0.316 ([#2364](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/2364)) | A base's fields, private ones included, would become wire and constructor-parameter names without its author's opt-in to synthesis, so an edit to that base would change a derived class's wire form; a hand-written base has chosen a wire contract a field-by-field derived form would bypass. Reopening bar: —(none stated); lifting the requirement is a widening that breaks no program. |
| Rejecting at compile time (or warning on) a derived class that declares components and inherits a synthesized `Serialize` without declaring `ISerializable` itself | `rejected` | spec §9.8 (v0.317); rationale §5.22; changelog v0.317 ([#5488](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5488)) | The inherited member's run-time type check already raises instead of dropping the components; a static rule would force every subclass of a serializable class to be serializable, a subclass with a non-defaultable, non-serializable field only by making that field nullable, and would leave a redeclaring subclass reached through a base-typed receiver unchecked. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Enforcing `serialized skip`'s and `serialized optional`'s defaultable-type requirement only where `Deserialize` is synthesized, so a write-only (`ISerializable`-only) class may exclude a non-defaultable component | `rejected` | spec §9.8 (*Field-name customization*); rationale §5.22 (v0.320 paragraph); changelog v0.320 ([#5497](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5497)) | Eligibility would depend on which directions a type declares, the direction-dependence §9.8 declines for a record's collection components and the v0.316 inheritance row declines for a base; the nullable form is the remedy. Reopening bar: —(none stated); lifting it is a widening that breaks no program. |
| Admitting a derived class's hand-written `Serialize` or `Deserialize` over a base's synthesized member: an exemption from §12.2 for a serialization interface the derived class lists (a class a base's `polymorphic` registry names excepted), with `base.Serialize(s)` exempt from §9.8's run-time type check | `rejected` | spec §12.2, §9.8 (*Inherited components*, v0.318); rationale §5.22 (v0.320 paragraph); changelog v0.320 ([#5505](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5505)) | It admits the static-type-selected shadow the *Member hiding* row declines (rationale §8.1); the `base.Serialize(s)` exemption relaxes §9.8's run-time type check, which applies however the member is reached; and the design leaves unstated the identity tag a `base.Serialize(s)` of `this` writes, with no read counterpart, `base.` being illegal in the static `Deserialize` (§12.3). A hierarchy that needs a form per class hand-writes the base's `Serialize` as `virtual`. Reopening bar: a design that settles the identity tag and the read counterpart without a shadow selected by static type. |
| Rejecting a serialized component whose static type is a class not `sealed`, or one that has derived classes, absent a `polymorphic` registry; writing a derived instance in its own form without a discriminator | `rejected` | spec §9.8 (v0.317); rationale §5.22; changelog v0.317 ([#5489](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5489)) | §9.8 makes `sealed` irrelevant to serialization and the stdlib's `Node` example uses a non-sealed component class; the derived set is not decidable under separate compilation; an undiscriminated derived form cannot be read by the static type's `Deserialize`. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Yielding `±Infinity` from binary-float `Parse`/`TryParse`/`ParseOrNull` and `Convert.ToDouble`/`TryToDouble` for an input spelling a finite number whose round-to-nearest-even value is not finite | `rejected` | stdlib §9.2, §2.3 (v0.313); spec §4.3 (v0.312); rationale §14.9; changelog v0.313 ([#5193](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5193)) | One digit string would be a compile error as a literal (§4.3) and an infinity as text; `decimal.Parse` (§9.3) and the integer `Parse` family fault where the number spelled does not fit the type; §2.3's `Convert` sentence would need a binary-float carve-out; and an input that spells a finite number the type cannot hold is a data problem the strict-fault model surfaces. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Leaving an interface's generic methods unconstrainable (no `where` clause on Appendix A.4 `IfaceMethod`/`IfaceStaticMethod`) | `rejected` | spec §14.2, Appendix A.4 (v0.314); changelog v0.314 ([#5248](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5248)) | §14.2's implementation clause would be vacuous (an interface member's inherited set could only be empty), and every constrained generic operation behind an interface would need an abstract class instead. No recorded reason favours it. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Requiring a method implementing a constrained interface generic method to restate the interface's `where` clauses exactly | `rejected` | spec §14.2 (v0.314); changelog v0.314 ([#5248](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5248)) | §14.2 already rules omit-or-restate-equivalent for overrides and interface implementations in one paragraph; a second rule for interfaces would split it, and an exact restatement adds no check the equivalence comparison lacks. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Requiring both members of a generic `owned`/plain pair to declare equal constraint sets for §22.6's declaration guard | `rejected` | spec §22.6 (v0.316); rationale §10; changelog v0.316 ([#5379](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5379)) | Each member's constraints are decided separately; a pair whose sets differ but both qualify (`where T: SomeClass` beside `where T: IDisposable`) collapses only at substitutions both members' constraints reject, so equality would reject a sound pair. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Giving a method that implements or overrides two generic declarations with non-equivalent constraint sets their union or intersection, or admitting a base-class witness whose set the interface method's set implies (a weaker witness) | `rejected` | spec §14.2, §11.2 (v0.314); changelog v0.314 ([#5248](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5248)) | A union adds constraints that go unenforced at calls through the weaker declaration (unsound). An intersection and a weaker witness are sound, but each is a widening relative to a declaration the method implements, which §14.2 rejects for a single declaration; admitting them would give the match a second, entailment-based relation that depends on where the witness is declared. Rejection is the conservative verdict: relaxing it later admits programs and rejects none. Reopening bar: a program needing one body behind declarations with differing sets that no restructuring at the base or the interface expresses. |
| Matching a generic method's own type parameters at an override or interface implementation by **name** rather than by position | `rejected` | spec §11.2 *Generic members*, §12.1 (v0.314), §14.1, §14.2; changelog v0.314 ([#5363](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5363)) | A method type parameter is a binder local to its declaration: matching by name would make renaming one a breaking change to every implementer and override, and with §14.1's rule that a method's type parameter does not reuse an enclosing one's name it would leave a `class C<T>` unable to implement or override any member declared `M<T>` at all. §14.2's constraint inheritance already pairs the two lists by position. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Admitting an override or interface implementation whose own type-parameter count differs from the matched declaration's (extra trailing parameters, or omitting ones the signature does not use) | `rejected` | spec §11.2 *Generic members*, §12.1 (v0.314), §14.5; changelog v0.314 ([#5363](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5363)) | A dispatched call supplies or infers exactly the matched declaration's type arguments: §14.5's explicit lists are all-or-nothing and a method type parameter takes no default (Appendix A `GenericParam`), so an extra parameter has nothing to bind it. A candidate with fewer either fixes a type the caller chooses (a non-generic `Show(x: T)` on `class C<T>`, reached through the interface with any type argument) or drops a parameter it does not use, which needs a correspondence that is not positional. Rejection is the conservative verdict: relaxing it later admits programs and rejects none. Reopening bar: defaults for method type parameters, or a recorded need restructuring cannot meet. |
| Bounding `double`/`float` literals by magnitude (anything above the largest finite value out of range) rather than by IEEE 754 round-to-nearest-even | `rejected` | spec §4.3, §5.2 (v0.312), §6.3; changelog v0.312 ([#5181](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5181)), v0.311 ([#5168](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5168)) | One value would get two verdicts by spelling (`1.7976931348623158e308` rejected, `double.MaxValue + 9e291` = `double.MaxValue` by §6.3), `float.MaxValue`'s own printed digits (`3.4028235e38f`) would be a compile error, and §4.3's float half would read against the decimal half v0.311 decided; it would also newly reject what the compiler admits. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Re-applying §19.3's reference-pattern rejection (or §12.7's reference-target rejection) at the instantiation site of a type-parameter pattern or target whose substitution is a reference type, over a concrete or substituted value | `rejected` | spec §19.3, §12.7, Appendix C.1 (v0.312), §3.6 rule 2; changelog v0.312 ([#5182](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5182)), v0.311 ([#5170](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5170)) | An instantiation-site rejection tracks the box, not the answer: only `as` and a binding would form one, and they are rejected; the bare test is answered as #5156 answers the same instantiated pair from the scrutinee side. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| A static `false` (or `null`) for every test between a value and a type-parameter pattern or target at a reference substitution | `rejected` | spec §12.7, §19.3 (v0.312); changelog v0.312 ([#5182](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5182)), v0.310 ([#5157](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5157)) | It answers `v is Object` as `false` for a value that is an `Object` boxed, disagrees with §12.7's carrier rule and #5156 on the same pair, and admits the `as` and binding forms whose true answer is the implicit box. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| A generic method's type parameter reusing (shadowing) the name of a type parameter in scope at its declaration (its enclosing type's or generic extension block's) — C#'s CS0693-warned hiding, the method's parameter winning throughout the method | `rejected` | spec §14.1, §26.4, Appendix C.1 (v0.314); rationale §10; changelog v0.314 ([#5227](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5227), [#5257](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5257), [#5264](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5264)) | A type parameter has no qualified spelling, so the shadow leaves the enclosing parameter unnameable in the method while the members it types stay in reach — two types spelled alike in one body (§21.10's local-type shadowing ground, sharpened). Renaming is always local, and an override's type parameters correspond to its base's by position, not by name (§11.2, §12.1). Reopening bar: a qualified spelling for an enclosing type parameter, or a recorded need renaming cannot meet. |
| Excluding a method's own type-parameter count from §11.3's distinguishing facts, so that `M<T>(x: T)` beside `M<T, U>(x: T)`, and the stdlib's `Task.FromException<T>` beside the non-generic `FromException`, clash; and making a pair that differs only by permuting its method type parameters clash | `rejected` | spec §11.3, §14.5, §22.6 (v0.314); rationale §10; changelog v0.314 ([#5364](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5364)) | An explicit list's length selects among the counts, and without a list a candidate whose type parameter nothing binds fails inference and drops out (spec §14.5), so each member stays reachable; the stdlib's `FromException`/`FromCancelled` pairs already rely on it (spec §30.10, stdlib §6.7). A permuted pair is told apart by an explicit list naming two different types. Reopening bar: a call shape at which a count-distinguished member cannot be selected. |
| A generic type's bare simple name inside its own declaration denoting the current instantiation (`Counter.n` in `class Counter<T>` as `Counter<T>.n` — C++'s injected-class-name), as a §2.7 rule-1 entry or as a fall-through | `rejected` | spec §14.1, §22.12 (v0.311), §2.2 *Collisions* (v0.310); changelog v0.311 ([#5164](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5164)); v0.303's raw-reference row | Every reference fixes its arity by its own tokens (§2.2, §14.1), and the self-name would be a reference whose tokens do not, outranking a same-identifier arity-0 type; it would hide a non-generic companion (`TaskCompletionSource` beside `TaskCompletionSource<T>`) inside the generic twin's body; the fall-through form lets an added import silently change a resolved chain (§2.7). `Counter<T>.n` and the bare member `n` already express it. Reopening bar: a use neither `G<T>.m` nor the bare member can express. |
| Rendering `Object` unqualified in `Type.FullName` and the §8.5 default (a stdlib §2.13 rule-(2)-style exemption), or giving the root no declaring module | `rejected` | spec §8.1, §2.3, §3.4 (v0.311), §2.8.3 rule 3; stdlib §2.13 (v0.311); changelog v0.311 ([#5165](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5165)) | Rule (2)'s ground is a canonical keyword, which `Object` lacks (§3.4); §2.8.3 rule 3 keys every type's identity on its declaring module. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Same-scope rebinding of a local's or parameter's name: a second local of one block, or a local at the top of a callable's body, reusing it (Rust's `let x = x.trim();`; `var s := s!;` after `var s: string?`) | `rejected` | spec §21.1 *Local names*, §17.15, Appendix C.5 (v0.314); rationale §8.1; changelog v0.314 ([#5370](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5370)) | Both bindings would be live in one scope under one spelling, and a local or parameter has no qualified spelling, so the earlier binding, possibly holding a different ownership obligation, becomes unreachable; a new name carries the rebinding (`using var r := move reader;`). Nested shadowing has its own rows. Reopening bar: a recorded need a fresh name cannot meet. |
| Nested shadowing: a declaration (a nested-block local, a loop variable, an arm binding, a binding-atom clause, a lambda's parameter, slot or local) reusing the name of a local or parameter in scope at it, the inner binding hiding the outer for the rest of the inner scope (Rust's, Swift's and Kotlin's reading) | `rejected` | spec §21.1 *Local names*, §17.15, §27.5, §22.10, Appendix C.5 (v0.316); rationale §8.1; changelog v0.316 ([#5382](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5382)) | The outer binding stays live, possibly holding an ownership obligation, with no qualified spelling, so it is unreachable for the rest of the inner scope; the name rules inside a declaration already refuse a shadow (§8.1's member hiding, §21.5's labels, §21.10's local types, §14.1's type parameters), while the shadows admitted at module level (§2.2, §2.3, §26.4) leave the hidden name reachable by qualification; and the shadow cost the compiler at least six defects. Sibling reuse stays legal. Reopening bar: a recorded need a fresh name cannot meet. |
| Admitting nested shadowing across a lambda boundary only: a lambda's parameters, slots and locals hiding an enclosing local or parameter (C# from version 8, whose design notes of 2021-11-03 record regret that it admitted a name both captured and shadowed) | `rejected` | spec §21.1 *Local names*, §27.5 (v0.316); rationale §8.1; changelog v0.316 ([#5382](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5382)) | A lambda captures by reference and a captured owned binding is a borrow of it (§27.5, §28.3); hiding a capture candidate inside the construct whose captures the borrow rules reason about is the case the rule exists for (the lambda rows of #1566, #3755 and #5267). Reopening bar: a recorded need a fresh name cannot meet. |

Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Restricting a record's synthesized `Equals(T)` witness to a *directly* listed `IEquatable<Self>` (an obligation reached through an extending interface needing a written `Equals`), or extending it to a non-Self `IEquatable<U>` | `rejected` | spec §14.2 *Interface conformance* clause 2 (v0.309); changelog v0.309 ([#5104](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5104)) | Clause 2's "lists I (or any interface extending I)" and "auto-generated members count" draw no direct/indirect line; the non-Self boundary follows from signature matching (§11.2), not from the listing path. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| An explicitly imported module's same-named public shadowing an always-available (§2.3) bare name ("the import wins"), the reverse ("the always-available name wins"), or a per-row split | `rejected` | spec §2.3 (v0.309), §2.2 *Collisions*, §2.7 rule 1; changelog v0.309 ([#5004](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5004)) | Either precedence lets an import (or a standard-library addition) silently re-bind a bare name that already resolved, which §2.7's closing guarantee rules out; §2.2 names a current-module declaration as the only shadower of an auto-imported name, and §2.3 gives every shadowable row the same *Yes*. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| A complete-group carve-out for operators declared to witness a static-interface constraint (an `INumber<T>`/`ISteppable<T>` conformer declaring `<` alone) — the coordinator's proposed direction, withdrawn at drafting | `rejected` | stdlib §2.19 (v0.309); spec §25.2 *Type-parameter operands*; changelog v0.181, v0.309 ([#4754](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4754)) | §25.2 derives the constrained generic body's entitlement to `>`, `<=`, `>=` from the group rule binding every conformer (the v0.181 decision); a lone-`<` conformer would leave `a > b` in a `where T: INumber<T>` body with nothing to bind at instantiation (the #1498 crash). The witness is an ordinary operator, reachable outside the constraint. Reopening bar: a proposal that re-grounds the entitlement and supplies the instantiation-time rejection a lone-`<` conformer would need. |
| Widening `INumber<T>`/`ISteppable<T>` to declare the full comparison group | `rejected` | stdlib §2.19 (v0.309); changelog v0.309 ([#4754](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4754)) | Restates as interface members an obligation §25.2 already imposes on every conformer; saves no conformer a declaration. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Reading `x!` over an owned binding as a borrow at an owned sink (so that `this.f := x!` must be spelled `this.f := move x!`), or admitting both spellings there | `rejected` | spec §17.8 (v0.309), §17.4, §28.3 *Producers*; changelog v0.309 ([#4993](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4993)) | §17.4's borrowed-`as` ground is a nullable result, which `!` lacks, and the reading would make `move x!` the only spelling at a marker-free position — the inversion §28.3's one-spelling-per-site rule names as an error. Reopening bar: a soundness counterexample in which `x!` at a marker-free position leaves two holders of one obligation. |
| A borrow stored through a `ref`/`out` slot into a caller location admitted under residual trust (checked mode as the backstop) | `rejected` | spec §28.3 *Borrow lifetime rules* (v0.309); changelog v0.309 ([#4819](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4819)) | K2.3 rejects an escape wherever scopes are statically comparable, and a `ref`/`out` location's scope is as comparable as the direct assignment's; a trust clause there would be an unrecorded K4.3 admission. Rejecting the callee-side store of a parameter-sourced borrow, and pooling only the plain arguments, are declined in the same record. Reopening bar: a `ref`/`out` location kind whose lifetime the caller cannot compare. |
| Reading a value-typed `is`/`as` operand against a reference target as statically true with an implicit box (so `p is IShape` is true and `p as IShape` allocates) | `rejected` | spec §12.7 (v0.309), §19.3; changelog v0.309 ([#4866](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4866)), v0.221 (#1984) | The test has no discriminating power, `as` would carry the hidden allocation #1984 declined for patterns, and `is` would disagree with `case` on the same question; the explicit box is one binding. Reopening bar: a use in which the implicitly boxed answer is not statically decided. |
| Reading a value-typed operand as never an instance of a reference type (every such `is` statically false) | `rejected` | spec §12.7 (v0.309), §8.1; changelog v0.309 ([#4866](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4866)) | Contradicts §8.1's universal root and answers opposite to the explicit box `(var o: Object := p; o is IShape)`. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Truncating decimal arithmetic (at an operand-derived or a maximal scale), or ties away from zero | `rejected` | spec §6.4 (v0.309), §4.4; changelog v0.309 ([#4872](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4872)) | Loses every significant digit where a full-precision quotient is representable (`1m / 1.0000000000000000000000000001m` = 0) and makes an operator the one decimal digit reduction that disagrees with `Round` and the format specifiers at a midpoint (stdlib §9.3, §2.18). Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Decimal results as the nearest value over the whole value set (instead of rounding at the largest fitting scale) | `rejected` | spec §6.4 (v0.309), §4.4 (v0.310); changelog v0.309 ([#4872](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4872)), v0.310 ([#5148](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5148)) | Differs only just above a (2^96 − 1)/10^(s+1) boundary, departs from the .NET rule the representation mirrors, and buys nothing a program can rely on; the literal and `Parse` sites take the same rule (§4.4, v0.310, [#5148](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5148)); `decimal(x)` rounds its shortest round-trip form by the same rule (§5.2, v0.311, [#5169](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5169)). Reopening bar: a program whose correctness depends on the boundary case. |
| The auto-generated primary as a *peer* §9.8 step-2 `Deserialize` candidate beside a record's explicit constructors | `rejected` | spec §9.8 (v0.309); changelog v0.309 ([#4775](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4775)) | A heterogeneous positional record's validating body constructor (`constructor(y: string, x: int)` beside `record P(X: int, Y: string)`) binds the same component set, so a peer primary would turn an accepted conformance into "more than one matches"; the primary is the fallback when no explicit constructor matches. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Arithmetic on `char` operands through §5.1's `char` → `int` widening (`c + 1`, `c * 2`, `ch - '0'`, a `char` shift count), and the scaling compound forms `c *= n`, `c /= n`, `c mod= n` on a `char` target | `rejected` | spec §3.5, §6.6, §17.6 (v0.309); changelog v0.309; owner ruling on [#5067](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5067) (2026-09-29) | `char` is not an arithmetic type; the arithmetic is spelled on `int(c)`, and a `char` target keeps only the code-point advance `+=`/`-=` (what `c++`/`c--` desugar to) with its checked write-back. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Admitting a `this(...)` chain cycle as a runtime non-termination, or detecting only syntactic self-chains | `rejected` | spec §12.8, Appendix C.5 (v0.309); changelog v0.309 ([#5036](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5036)) | §22.8 runs field initializers in the constructor at the end of the chain and §22.7 credits a chaining constructor with the chained-to constructor's §7.9 work; around a cycle that credit is circular, so §7.9 is discharged by nothing. A self-edge can appear only after resolution. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Rejecting a consume of an owning match's whole-value arm binding (`var y => do var z := y; end`) | `rejected` | spec §35.5 (v0.309); changelog v0.309 ([#4821](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4821)) | The owning match has consumed the value, so the binding names a move-only value K3.12 moves soundly; the rejection would make the one binding of a consuming construct unable to consume, for no soundness gain. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| A §28.3 row-4 carve-out admitting a plain static return of a trivial-dispose or builtin collection class (`static method F(): Array<int>` as a borrow of runtime-managed storage) — the coordinator's proposed direction, withdrawn at drafting | `rejected` | spec §28.3 row 4, §3.1, §28.9, K1.6 (v0.309); changelog v0.309 ([#5123](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5123)); the `retired` trivial-dispose reclaim row below | `Array<T>` is a class type (§3.1) and trivial-dispose, not runtime-managed (§28.9's tier is exhaustive); it carries the free obligation (K1.6), so a sourceless static return names no responsible party. The carve-out would reinstate the retired reclaim. Reopening bar: a proposal reopening the retired reclaim row with new evidence against its reasons. |
| Rejecting a base class's static member named through a derived type (`D<string>._c` where only `B<T>` declares `_c`) | `rejected` | spec §22.12 (v0.309); changelog v0.309 ([#4974](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4974)) | §2.7's enclosing-type rule counts inherited statics and §12.2 makes inherited members visible at the derived declaration site; the derived spelling names the declaring type's cell, one cell per declaring instantiation. Reopening bar: a recorded case where aliasing the declaring cell is unsound. |
| An F-bounded (recurrence) reading of a type alias whose `where` clause names the alias (`type Sorted<T> := Array<T> where T: Sorted<T>`) | `rejected` | spec §27.8 (v0.308); changelog v0.308 ([#4783](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4783)) | An alias is a synonym with no identity apart from its expansion, so there is no nominal type for the bound to recur on; §11.8's recurrence names a declared interface. Reopening bar: aliases acquiring an identity of their own (a nominal or exported alias form), at which point the question is re-asked of that form. |
| Parameter names (case-sensitive or not) distinguishing a body constructor from the primary under §9.3/§11.1 | `rejected` | spec §9.3, §11.1, §11.3 (v0.308); changelog v0.308 ([#4775](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4775)) | Two constructors with identical type lists are indistinguishable at every call (§22.6 has no stage that separates them), so the names reading admits a declaration no positional construction can call; §11.3's clash rule already governs. Reopening bar: a named-argument-only construction form that could select between them. |
| Per-slot definite assignment of an inline-array local (element stores assigning an unassigned array piecewise) | `rejected` | spec §15.1 (v0.308), §7.9, §28.3 K1; changelog v0.308 ([#5026](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5026)) | Definite assignment is per local and a dynamic index cannot be tracked per slot; the whole-array assignment is the one assigning form. Reopening bar: a program shape that cannot be written as a whole-array assignment (for example, slots whose values are only available one at a time across a loop with a non-constant bound, and no defaultable or nullable element form serving). |
| A windowed checked-disposal quarantine (free a disposed block after N later disposals and reuse it) as the bound on the default build's retention | `rejected` | rationale §5.30 (*Checked disposal mode is the default*, v0.307); [proposal 0128 §3](0128-checked-disposal-default.md) | —(none stated). Reclaiming or reusing a block after N disposals turns §28.2's "raises" and K4.1's "faults deterministically" into a windowed, probabilistic fault — the unchecked relaxation in disguise; the recorded bound is per-instance retention limited to the flag's footprint, never reuse of a live instance's address. |
| Raw and open generic type references (a bare `Box` at a `class Box<T>`, inside `Box<T>`'s own declaration as outside it; tolerated today at ordinary generic types) | `rejected` | spec §14.1 (v0.303, v0.311), §22.12 (v0.311), Appendix C.1; changelog v0.303, v0.311 | A type's program-wide identity is keyed on arity (§2.8.1), so a reference that supplies none names no type; and there is nothing at a type reference to infer arity *from*, which is why §14.5's omit-or-supply-in-full rule is scoped to a generic member call. Reopening bar: a use for an arity-less generic type name — a reflection surface over an uninstantiated generic, or a variance-free `typeof(G<>)` form — together with the identity rule it would need. |
| A required diagnostic withheld at an inert `static invariant` (accept-and-silently-skip with no signal) | `rejected` | spec §22.16 (v0.303); changelog v0.303 | The clause is a prove-time obligation with no prover to discharge it and no run-time stand-in, so `static invariant(false);` compiles and does nothing while reading exactly like a checked predicate. Reopening bar: the prover fragment landing — at which point the warning is withdrawn rather than silenced, this being the condition the rule itself names. |
| Keying the use-after-dominating-`Dispose()` diagnostic on the use form (a consume an error where a read is a warning) | `rejected` | spec §28.2 (v0.303), §28.3; changelog v0.303 | §28.2's list has named read, borrow and consume together since it was written, and pairs the dominating kill with the conditional one as the contrast that decides severity. A verdict that varies with where the use sits syntactically describes one program state two ways. Reopening bar: a soundness argument that a *dominating* consume is more dangerous than a *dominating* read — the checked-disposal backstop covers both today — plus a statement of how the cascade-suppression contract survives it. |
| Abstract and `closed` classes admitted at `where T: new()`, with the rejection left to `new T()` in the generic body | `rejected` | spec §14.2 (v0.302), §12.5, Appendix C.4; changelog v0.302 | The constraint's only purpose is `new T()`, which §12.5 makes a compile error on such a class, so the admitting reading satisfies a constraint the language then refuses to honour — and diagnoses it inside a generic body the author may not own rather than at the substitution they wrote. Reopening bar: a use for `where T: new()` at an unconstructible class that does not go through `new T()` — the constraint would then be carrying a different guarantee and should say which. |
| A nullability carve-out from §17.13's `Task`-family discard prohibition (admitting `_ := someNullableTask;`) | `rejected` | spec §17.13 (v0.302); §28.3's announcement-table Keying rule; changelog v0.302 | The prohibition is about abandonment, not leaks, and a non-null `Task?` abandons exactly the operation a `Task` does; the neighbouring keying rule strips nullability for the same family. Reopening bar: a stated reason why a nullable static type should change what happens to the operation behind a present reference. |
| Per-element arrival tracking in the ordered owning collections (an insertion-keyed disposal order that survives `Insert` and `Sort`) | `rejected` | spec §28.11 (v0.302); stdlib §4.3, §4.5; changelog v0.302 | These types store elements, not arrival order: after `OwningArray.Sort` or `Reverse` the insertion order is not recoverable from the collection at all, so an insertion-keyed rule names an order no conforming implementation can produce. Making it producible costs a per-element field in every ordered owning collection. Reopening bar: a program whose correctness depends on insertion order rather than storage order at disposal, plus a storage cost the project accepts. |
| Keying the choice reserved-name set on classification (`Equals`/`GetHashCode`/`ToString` free as case names on a non-auto-hashable choice) | `rejected` | spec §35.1 (v0.302), §35.7, §3.6; changelog v0.302 | §35.7's stated remedy for the non-auto-hashable use-site error is to *declare* the member, which a case holding that name makes unspellable — so the classification-keyed reading withdraws the remedy from exactly the choices that need it, and makes §3.6's auto-hashable flip legalize or outlaw a case name as an unlisted side effect. Reopening bar: a §3.6 flip list that carries case-name legality as a recorded consequence, plus a remedy for the non-auto-hashable use-site error that does not require the name. |
| Retiring `Semaphore`'s two-argument constructor (`maxCount` as vestigial, at a subtraction review) | `rejected` | stdlib §6.3 (v0.302); changelog v0.302; process §10 | `maxCount` was a candidate for subtraction because the token discipline makes a permit ceiling unreachable after construction. Reopening bar: a subtraction review that takes the whole two-argument form, with the migration for callers that pass it today. |
| Reading `TaskScope`'s "first fault" as spawn order | `rejected` | stdlib §6.10 (v0.302); rationale §13.5's no-aggregation posture; changelog v0.302 | Spawn order fixes nothing about a set of children already running concurrently, and pinning it would oblige the scope to order fault delivery rather than report it. The load-bearing guarantee — exactly one fault re-raised, never an aggregate, siblings cancelled — holds without it. Reopening bar: a construction that makes spawn-order selection observable without serializing the children, or a program that needs deterministic selection among concurrent faults. |
| Property-backed storage for a capture-bearing function value (a `scoped` or `owned` property, or a release rule for an auto-property's backing field) | `rejected` | spec §28.3 (*property and indexer stores*), §23.1 (v0.314); rationale §4.2 (*Function values in properties and indexers*); changelog v0.314 ([#5303](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5303)) | A storage tier on properties that fields lack: a property declares neither modifier, a plain field admits only a capture-free function value, and the `scoped` field's bounding-object admission needs a call site a setter does not have. Reopening bar: a `scoped` property design that resolves a bounding object at an assignment site, together with the same tier for fields. |
| Reading a function-typed property assignment by accessor form (an auto-property's as a field store, a block-form setter's `value` as a call-bounded parameter) | `rejected` | spec §28.3 (*property and indexer stores*), §23.3 (v0.314); rationale §4.2 (*Function values in properties and indexers*); changelog v0.314 ([#5303](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5303)) | An interface or `virtual` site cannot see which form the dispatch reaches, and an auto-property implements an interface property, so the split leaves a capture-bearing closure in a backing field; closing that seam by barring a function-typed auto-property from implementing an interface or `virtual` property leaves a block-form setter unable to store at all. Reopening bar: a way for an assignment site to know the accessor form it reaches. |
| Classifying a record as borrow-bearing while a function-typed component holds a capture-bearing value (classification by contents) | `rejected` | spec §28.3 (*record components of function type*), §9.7 (v0.316); rationale §4.2 (*Function values in record components and primary-constructor captures*); changelog v0.316 ([#5377](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5377)) | §9.7 classifies by declared field types, and §3.6 rule 1 forbids a classification that follows the values a record holds; a declared function type is runtime-managed. Reopening bar: a value-keyed classification that §3.6 and the artifact interface (§2.8.2) can carry. |
| Judging a primary constructor's capture of a plain function-typed parameter at the construction site (the reading a record's generated constructor takes) | `rejected` | spec §22.10, §28.14 (v0.316); rationale §4.2 (*Function values in record components and primary-constructor captures*); changelog v0.316 ([#5378](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5378)) | Whether the parameter becomes a field depends on whether some body references it (capture-iff-used), so the caller's verdict would key on a body it cannot see and change when a method is added or removed. Reopening bar: a signature-visible marker that the parameter is stored, which a regular constructor with a declared field already provides. |
| Reading a property assignment of a borrow-carrying value by the accessor form the site resolves (an auto-property's as a field store, a block-form setter's as a plain-parameter call) | `rejected` | spec §28.3 (*property stores of a borrow-carrying value*), §23.3 (v0.316); rationale §5.24 (v0.316 addendum on property assignments); changelog v0.316 ([#5387](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5387)) | An interface or `virtual` site cannot see which form the dispatch reaches, and an auto-property implements an interface property, so one object draws two verdicts and a fresh value admitted through an interface dangles in the backing field it reaches. Reopening bar: as for the function-valued row above, an assignment site that can know which accessor form it reaches. |
| Reading every property assignment of a borrow-carrying value as a call of its setter (the plain-parameter residual for an auto-property's store) | `rejected` | spec §28.3 (*property stores of a borrow-carrying value*), §23.1 (v0.316); rationale §5.24 (v0.316 addendum on property assignments); changelog v0.316 ([#5387](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5387)) | An auto-property's assignment is a store to its backing field, so this would turn §28.3's static rejection of a scope-bound local's borrow in a field and §28.6's fresh-store error into a trust residual for the commonest property form. Reopening bar: a property form whose assignment provably reaches no storage, declared as such. |
| Rejecting, at a function value's body, a borrow it returns from its own borrow-bearing record, inline-array or holds-borrows choice parameter (instead of counting the argument's carried borrows at the invocation) | `rejected` | spec §28.3 (announcement table row 4; borrowed-field clause 2) (v0.317); changelog v0.317 ([#5485](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5485)) | *Concrete-slot function bodies* and the bare-type-parameter callee side both admit a borrow reached through a plain parameter; carving value-typed parameters out would reject the ordinary selector `lambda(s) => s.Item` and split the two admissions, where the caller re-establishes the carried borrows as the `ref`/`out` rule already counts them. Reopening bar: a selector shape the caller-side count over-rejects. |
| Banning borrow-bearing record, inline-array and holds-borrows choice parameters on synchronous iterator methods, as §30.1 bans them on the `async` kinds (instead of counting the borrows such an argument carries among the returned frame's sources) | `rejected` | spec §28.3 (*Deferred-activation callees*; *Returning a deferred-activation frame*), §29.4 (v0.317); changelog v0.317 ([#5485](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5485)) | Counting is the rule reference-typed arguments already take; a ban would reject the sound case of a frame consumed within its source's scope. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Classifying a choice or inline array as holding borrows while a function-typed payload or slot holds a capture-bearing value (classification by contents) | `rejected` | spec §28.3 (*choice payloads and inline-array slots of function type*) (v0.317); rationale §4.2 (*Function values in choice payloads and inline-array slots*); changelog v0.317 ([#5486](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5486)) | As for records (#5377): §35.4 and §3.6 classify by declared types, and §3.6 rule 1 forbids a classification that follows the values held. Reopening bar: a value-keyed classification that §3.6 and the artifact interface (§2.8.2) can carry. |
| An instantiation exclusion for generic types declaring a `T`-typed field, auto-property or record component, at function-type substitutions (proposal 0133 Option 2a) | `rejected` | spec §28.3 (*Borrow lifetime rules*, the closure bullet's set-erasing positions) (v0.320); rationale §4.2 (*Function values at set-erasing positions*); [proposal 0133](0133-set-erasing-positions.md) §3, §7; changelog v0.320 ([#5375](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5375)) | Keyed on fields it rejects capture-free `Holder<() -> int>` uses, does not reach a `Bag<T>` storing into an `Array<T>` field, and does not reach `Object`. | Rejecting, in a generic body, a store of a bare-type-parameter value into a field, collection, `ref`/`out` location or return unless the constraints exclude function types (proposal 0133 Option 3) | `rejected` | spec §28.3 (*Borrow lifetime rules*, the closure bullet's set-erasing positions) (v0.320); rationale §4.2 (*Function values at set-erasing positions*); [proposal 0133](0133-set-erasing-positions.md) §3, §7; changelog v0.320 ([#5375](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5375)) | `where T: class`, the one constraint admitting every class and interface type, admits function types (§14.2), so every unconstrained or `class`-constrained container would lose its own element type; it does not reach `Object`. | Exempting `EventSource<T>.Raise`/`RaiseAsync` by name at a function-type payload (a per-member set-erasing allowlist) | `rejected` | spec §28.3 (*Borrow lifetime rules*, the closure bullet's set-erasing positions) (v0.320); rationale §4.2 (*Function values at set-erasing positions*); [proposal 0133](0133-set-erasing-positions.md) §3, §7; changelog v0.320 ([#5375](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5375)) | The first allowlist outside §28.14's retention register; a user `IEvent<T>` implementation could not share it, and raising data is the remedy. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |

Reopening bar: a derived property or constraint that states a rendering requirement. |

The rejection would be an interior diagnostic with no signature-side remedy, and would refuse through `Result<T, E>` the value a bare `T` renders at the same substitution. Reopening bar: a derived property or constraint that states a rendering requirement. |
| A `ToArray()` on `ReadOnlyArraySpan<T>`, riding the v0.272 collection-literal carrier fix | `rejected` (the `ToArray()` spelling; **the value arrived at v0.294 at the witness spelling** — `Array<T>.FromCollectionLiteral(elements: ReadOnlyArraySpan<T>): owned Array<T>`, the static witness spec §11.8 requires of a declared `ICollectionLiteralTarget<Array<T>, T>` conformer, structural rather than *while-we're-at-it*; D-0009, [proposal 0125 §2.3](0125-static-interface-promotions.md)) | [proposal 0115 §7 R3](0115-collection-literal-borrowing-span-family.md); changelog v0.272; changelog v0.294 | A stdlib pass that takes it on its own merits — for the `ToArray()` spelling, which stays declined as a dual of the witness. The asymmetry is real — `ReadOnlySpan<T>` declares `ToArray(): owned Array<T>` at value `T` and `ReadOnlyArraySpan<T>` does not, so a factory body spelled `elements.ToArray()` stops compiling — but in-tree uses are zero and external users are zero, and a stdlib addition riding a signature fix is the *while-we're-at-it* expansion the project's operating rules forbid. Surface is debt; the remedy is a loop or an explicit `Array<T>`. ⚠ **The priced loss is now zero (v0.277):** this row's record — the v0.272 changelog — called the asymmetry *"the migration's one genuine loss"* because `ReadOnlySpan<T>` declared `ToArray()` and this family did not — but the compiler never implemented that member and rejected the call, so v0.277 deleted it. The families are symmetric, both by declaration and by behaviour. The row's **other** grounds are untouched and still carry the decline: zero in-tree uses, zero external users, and a stdlib addition riding a signature fix is the *while-we're-at-it* expansion the operating rules forbid. |
| Keying the local half of definite assignment on **nullability** rather than on **defaultability** | `rejected` | spec §7.9, §21.1, §28.3, Appendix C; changelog v0.299 ([#4362](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4362)) | Evidence that a defaultable non-nullable local read before assignment is a bug worth a diagnostic. §7.9's lead sentence said *non-nullable* where the section's own *Default initialization* split says *non-defaultable*, and `int` is both — so the two sentences gave opposite verdicts on `var n: int;` followed by a read. The split's vocabulary wins: it is the finer predicate, it is the one §7.9 defines, and the alternative deletes §15.1.1's own worked example and contradicts §15.1's *Initialization* subsection, which would make the strict reading a design change rather than a defect fix. The compiler is stricter than either v0.298 sentence (`src/sema/DefiniteAssignment.cpp:1122` marks a no-initializer local assigned only for inline arrays), so this is a spec-level loosening and a spec-ahead-of-impl delta. |
| Re-illustrating §17.2's mandatory-parenthesization rule with a bitwise × logical mix that type-checks | `rejected` | spec §17.2; changelog v0.299 ([#4369](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4369)) | An operand pairing that makes such a mix well-typed under some grouping. Two of §17.2's four illustrated mixes are ill-typed under **every** grouping, so a compiler that never implements §17.2 still rejects those programs and the illustration cannot demonstrate the rule it illustrates. The fix taken instead states that the rule is *syntactic* — a parenthesization restores a parse, not a whole program — which is true of all four mixes and needs no new example. Re-illustrating was declined because §17's operand tables admit no bitwise × logical pairing to build one from: the honest illustration of a syntax rule is a program that fails to parse. |
| Suppressing the must-use warning on a move-only value's implicit drop via `using var` | `rejected` | spec §15.3, §28.10, §35.4; kernel K1 rule 14; changelog v0.300 ([#4376](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4376)) | A rule, stated somewhere, defining that warning for owning **inline arrays**. Half of #4376's drafted ruling claimed `using var` over a move-only value suppresses a must-use warning on the implicit drop; the revision's composition gate withdrew it. No section defines the warning for that kind: K1 rule 14 scopes it to a plain-`var` local or an undischarged `owned` parameter, K1's *owned* is class- or interface-typed, §35.4 had to **state** the warning explicitly for owning **choices** — which is evidence it does not follow automatically for a move-only kind — and §28.10 says, unchanged by the same revision, that the only additional effect of `using var` on a move-only local is the reassignability restriction. A clarification may not create a diagnostic in a subordinate clause with no rule defining it and no remedy available. #4376's **adoption** ruling stands in full. **Foreclosed at v0.301:** the bar asked for a rule defining that warning for owning inline arrays; §15.3's *Local lifetime* bullet now states the opposite directly — no must-use warning attaches at an owning inline array's auto-drop — so the bar is answered in the negative rather than met, and introducing the warning is a design change from here, not a clarification. The owning **choice** half is untouched, §35.4 stating that warning and its `using var` suppression as before. |
| Writing the implementation's one-source-file compilation into the normative text | `rejected` | spec §2.4, §2.5, §2.6; changelog v0.300 ([#4384](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4384)) | Nothing — the spec is not wrong here, so there is nothing to reopen. The issue proposed stating that a compilation is one source file in the current implementation. Declined on two grounds: it is implementation narration in normative text, which the spec-change process forbids in terms, and it would make the normative document track the implementation — the reverse of `CLAUDE.md`'s first rule. §2.4 requires the compiler to take **all source files** of the compilation unit, §2.5 requires one invocation per SCC, and §2.6 declares build-tool integration implementation-defined; `bezelc`'s single-input driver is a compiler gap tracked as such. |
| A blanket rejection of a nullable left operand to `in` | `rejected` | spec §17.5, §16.6; changelog v0.270 (#3312.2) | A case where the derived rule gives the wrong answer. `x in c` *is* `c.Contains(x)`, so admissibility follows the ordinary argument check: rejected over every range (`Range<T>.Contains` takes a non-nullable `T`, and ordering admits no absent participant), admitted where the selected `Contains` parameter is itself nullable. A blanket rule would reject `n in arr` while `arr.Contains(n)` compiles — the *"same shape, opposite answers, decided by spelling"* defect proposal 0114 cited as its own motivation — and would contradict v0.267's D2, which retained §8.4 equality over absent operands. |
| A default-comparer `Sort`/`Sorted` supplied by an extension on `Array<T>` or `OwningArray<T>` | `rejected` | stdlib §4.4, §4.5; spec §26.5 rule 1; changelog v0.274 ruling 2; [#3324](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/3324) | A design decision on how to spell natural-order sorting, taken on its own. The extension form is **unreachable by construction**: §26.5 rule 1 commits instance resolution by name *of any arity*, and both types declare `Sort`/`Sorted`, so extension members of those names are never consulted. Renaming them (`SortNatural`/`SortedNatural`) was declined as inventing public vocabulary inside a correction sweep — that part stands. **The rest of this row was wrong and v0.275 reverses it:** the closing clause read *"the unreachable declarations were deleted, losing no capability because there was none"*, and the capability **ships** — `Array<T>` parameterless `Sort`/`Sorted` carry a sema admissibility gate, lowering, cross-module paths, twelve test files and five hardening issues including one measured silent-wrong-answer bug. The compiler never implemented it through the extension, so §26.5's argument was sound about the *extension* and unsound about the *capability*. v0.275 restores the documentation as members of `Array<T>` with instantiation-checked admissibility. What remains declined is only the **extension** spelling, which is genuinely unreachable. |
| A generic `Math.Abs<T>(x: T): T where T: INumber<T>` | `rejected` | rationale §15.y (v0.290); [proposal 0123 §5 item 2](0123-stdlib-regularization-batch-2026-09.md); stdlib §2.2 | Not call-site-invisible: `float` → `double` is a `float` argument's one applicable spec §5.1 conversion among the four parameter types, so `Math.Abs(floatVal)` resolves to `Abs(double): double` today (spec §22.6) and would return `float` under the generic — a result-type change at every `var` and every follow-on — and that mapping is the very block `DECLINED.md`'s `Average` row records; the `pure` loss comes with it. Reopen on a result-type facility on `INumber<T>` (the `Average` row's own bar). (Premise corrected v0.317, #5490: the row cited `Math.Abs(shortVal)` resolving to `Abs(int): int`; under spec §22.6's co-equal ranking a `short`/`sbyte` argument converts to all four parameter types and is ambiguous, so the generic would admit that call rather than change it. The `float` case carries the ground; the decision is unchanged.) |
| A per-type `readonly` assignable context (a constructor of `R` may write another `R` value's `readonly` storage) | `rejected` | spec §22.8, §23.1, §9.9; changelog v0.270 (#3299) | Evidence that the capability is needed by a real shape. It is unsound as stated: §7.4 licenses narrowing on a `readonly` field by asserting *"no writer on any path, through any alias"* outside the construction window, and a cross-instance constructor write is exactly such a writer reached by a call — §7.4's invalidation list is closed and a plain borrow argument is not on it, so a narrowed non-null read returns null. Three sweeps of `docs/` and `tests/` found zero occurrences of the shape outside proposal 0107's own attack record, so the restriction costs nothing to adopt. |
| A recurrence-constrained `IDeserializable<TSelf>` (#3339's shape) | `rejected` | [proposal 0125 §3.1, §3.7](0125-static-interface-promotions.md) | —(none stated). Cannot express the `owned Shared<C>` entry without a foreign-member bridge. |
| Move-only choice elements in the §4.14 buffer/span family (candidate C3 of the v0.275 ruling) | `rejected` | stdlib §4.14; spec §35, §15.3; changelog v0.274; [#3322](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/3322) | A ruling on admissibility. §4.14's taxonomy was pinned at v0.102 and never re-read against §35; choices became move-only at v0.200. v0.274 corrected the **move-only** count (the stdlib said the kinds were the owning inline arrays alone where §15.3 says they are two) but left the **value-typed** lists alone, because a *value-like* choice matching neither the class bullet, the value bullet, nor the exclusion list is a gap whose closing decides what is admitted. |
| Admitting `SortedDictionary<K, V>` as a dictionary-literal target by silent implementation | `rejected` | changelog v0.288 (#3517); spec §15.4 | A proposal that widens §15.4's two fresh-store whitelists on their merits — #3489's question, not decided at v0.288. | Aggregating handler exceptions (running every handler and collecting the failures) | `rejected` | rationale §13.5 (*Handler exception policy: first error wins, not aggregate*) | —(none stated). Three reasons: aggregation forces a special exception type on every event consumer (`AggregateException`'s `Flatten`/`InnerExceptions`/`Handle` surface); events are a fire-once/many-respond shape where the publisher "cares that *something* failed"; and handlers needing independence opt in trivially. **Verdict vocabulary is implicit** ("was considered … Bezel chose first-wins for three reasons"). |
| An explicit bar on §5 materializers over owning-collection receivers | `rejected` | stdlib §4.5; changelog v0.266; rationale §18 *LINQ over owned-element collections* | The form is sound (a borrowing snapshot), §26.5 has no blocking mechanism short of the type declaring the name, and resolution on the compile-time type means any bar is defeated by an upcast. Reopening bar: a soundness demonstration, plus a construction that cannot be laundered through `IEnumerable<T>`. |

| `ArgumentException`-compatible contract-violation type | `rejected` | rationale §9.8 | —(none stated). Declined for lack of an honest `ParamName` mapping on multi-term conditions; the per-member migration cost is recorded and borne deliberately. |

The condition is an overflow of the tick domain — the §31.4 `OverflowException` class `TimeSpan`'s arithmetic already raises — re-classified from the band-1 defect surface its `Argument*` label implied to band 4's catchable fault; the constructors' component validation stays argument-blamed. |
| Arity mismatch at a function-typed slot as a hard error at the candidate (the call not surviving the mismatch) | `rejected` | spec §14.5 (phase 1's function-typed bullet, v0.296); spec §22.6 (*Parameter count at a function-typed position*); spec §27.1.1 (opening sentence); changelog v0.296 | —(none stated). Component-wise unification over unequal slot counts unifies nothing, and §14.5 already makes every inference failure a drop-out; an error at the candidate would make an overload set keyed on function-typed arity (proposal 0002 §3's family; stdlib §6.8's `Domain.Spawn` forms) unusable by design. |
| Assign-every-`out`-before-the-first-suspension (the alternative to banning `ref`/`out` in `async` and iterator bodies) | `rejected` | spec §30.1 (*`ref` and `out` parameters are forbidden* — the closing parenthetical) | —(none stated). "was considered and declined: it is coherent for `out` only and unsound for `ref` (the callee can *read* the dead slot after resumption, not just write it), fragile (inserting an `await` above the assignment breaks callers), and has no known use case; C# rejects both, for both method kinds." |

Reading the head is a target-directed inference §14.5 does not have (rationale §10) and would make one body's wrapper depend on which candidate is under trial; the blanket requirement over-rejects the value-typed body that binds `R` cleanly. Reopen with a stated agreement rule for a head-read binding, or a corpus program that must write a fresh-owned-class `async` lambda at a `Task<R>` slot without an ascription. |

Two narrowings declined in the same record: freezing explicit `Dispose()` too ("a de-facto borrow checker for no soundness gain"), and scoping the freeze to domain-crossing sites only ("leaky, not compositionally closed"). |

| Constraint-filtered overload applicability: an unsatisfied `where` constraint dropping a generic candidate from §22.6's set (C#'s rule since 7.3), so that `M(5)` beside `M(o: Object)` and `M<T>(x: T) where T: class` reaches the non-generic | `rejected` | spec §14.5, §22.6 (v0.316); rationale §10; changelog v0.316 ([#5406](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5406)) | Recorded as **liftable**: filtering only removes candidates, so it changes the outcome only of calls this rule rejects. Declined so that whether a constraint holds never decides which overload a call reaches: under filtering, a conformance edit (a type gaining an interface) that makes a candidate's constraints hold can re-route a call that already compiles. Cost: the class/value split (`M<T>(x: T) where T: class` beside `M<T>(x: T?) where T: value`) is spelled with distinct names. Reopening bar: programs that need the filter, weighed against that re-routing. |

Three grounds, any one fatal: aliases see through (spec §27.8), so it would buy range safety and not the nominal distinctness a services language needs; no conversion boundary exists to check at (spec §25.4), so the check would be the parallel mechanism spec §3.6's closing rule forbids; and aliases are module-internal. Its useful content is the record-invariant design (spec §22.16's recorded out-of-scope item). |

The whole-program-analysis variant "dies at module boundaries". |

One question (how a deserializer rebuilds a borrowing back-edge), three rejected answers: relaxing §7.9's definite assignment "reintroduces exactly the null-dereference class the language spent its nullability design eliminating … invisibly inside generated code"; topological ordering "handles a DAG but not a cycle" and makes construction order a function of the borrow graph; nullable borrowing fields push the deserializer's difficulty "onto every downstream use site, the worst possible place for it". |

Overwriting an admitted mutable static "never disposes the displaced referent — deliberately *not* §28.7's drop-on-overwrite, since a racing first use may still hold and await the displaced task"; the displaced referent living to process exit is "an accepted bounded cost of the same kind as the §22.13 exemption". |
| Duck-typed collection-literal enrollment (C#'s `IEnumerable<T>` + `Add` + parameterless-constructor fallback) | `rejected` | rationale §5.16 (*One opt-in path, not two*) | —(none stated). "Bezel deliberately does not replicate this": the spec keeps exactly one structural-dispatch rule (`Contains` for `in`), and a second path would be "accidental enrollment … your type became literal-constructible because it happens to have an `Add` method". C# carries the dual path for pre-existing-type compatibility Bezel does not have. |
| Eiffel-style side-effecting assertions, and a global assertion-suppression flag | `rejected` | rationale §9.8 | —(none stated). Clause purity is what makes the tier system coherent; both Eiffel mechanisms are named counter-examples. |
| Element-listing auto-`ToString` for inline arrays and the unit type | `rejected` | spec §8.5 (*`ToString`* — the inline-array bullet) | —(none stated). "element-listing output is deliberately not synthesized — an inline array's elements may be class-typed borrows whose formatting is not the container's business." The rendering is the type's name (`"[3]int"`, `"()"`). |
| Endianness and bit order chosen at run time (a platform-default accessor, an enum parameter, or a default) | `rejected` | stdlib §4.14 (*Endianness in the method name*; *Bit order and byte order in the method name*) | —(none stated). Every accessor names the axis: "There is no platform-default version and no enum parameter — both alternatives invite the class of bugs where someone forgets that 'ints over the network are big-endian' or where the same source produces different values on different platforms." The bit tier repeats it: "no bit-order parameter, no constructor argument, and no default." C#'s `BinaryPrimitives` is the cited convergent precedent. |
| Entry iteration on `OwningDictionary<K, V>` (a borrowing look-don't-take entry view) | `rejected` | stdlib §4.9 (*Default `for var entry in ownedDict do` iteration is not provided*) | —(none stated). Its common intent is ownership transfer, and "a borrowing look-don't-take entry view over an owning dictionary is a separate decision deliberately not taken here"; the sanctioned spellings are key-iteration (borrowing) or `Drain()` (transferring). |
| Enum-constant and choice-case serialized-name collisions read as outside §9.8's uniqueness clause — admitting `A serialized "x", B serialized "x"` under a first-declared-wins read rule, or carving same-valued aliases (§10.2) out of the clause | `rejected` | spec §9.8 (*Field-name customization*, the uniqueness sentence, v0.297); [#3863](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/3863); changelog v0.297 | —(none stated). The clause exists to keep the read direction unambiguous, and two constants under one wire name are the ambiguity itself; a first-declared-wins read would silently map a written `B` back to `A`. The alias carve fails on §9.8's own inert-annotation discipline: the write direction already uses the first-declared name for a shared value, so the second alias's rename could never be written and would be dead text that reads as load-bearing. |
| Enum-intrinsic ordering: `<`/`<=`/`>`/`>=` on enum operands with no declared `operator` group, by underlying value (C#) or by declaration position (Java, Swift's synthesized `Comparable`, Rust's derived `PartialOrd`) | `rejected` | spec §10.5 (*Ordering*), §3.5, Appendix C.10 (v0.314); rationale §9.4; changelog v0.314 ([#5365](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5365)) | A proposal that adds the ordering together with `IComparable<E>` conformance, names one order and its reading under §10.2 aliasing, and shows defects the explicit spellings cause. Every type with an intrinsic `<` also declares `IComparable<T>`, so an operator-only ordering would leave enums ordered by `<` and unordered to `Sort`/`OrderBy`. The two candidate orders disagree on non-ascending explicit values, and declaration order is undefined under aliasing. `int(a) < int(b)`, a declared group, or `IComparable<E>` states the order. |
| Enum-prefix omission in expression position (Swift-style leading-dot member lookup) | `rejected` | rationale §11.5 (v0.242; proposal 0086 item 5) | —(none stated). Pattern position has one scrutinee fixed by `of`; expression operands are peers, so a resolving rule "would make name resolution type-directed and bidirectional, which spec §2.7's position-keyed, no-backtracking design deliberately excludes". Swift's `.low` is buildable "at the cost of a dedicated sigil form and inference-driven member lookup pervading expression syntax … for the saving of one qualifier". |
| Enum value initializers: a sibling constant read as an enum value (so `C := A + 100` has no operator and an alias needs `int(A)`), no sibling references at all, or forward sibling references under cycle detection | `rejected` | spec §10.2 (*Sibling constants in value initializers*), §17.14 (v0.315); rationale §9.4; changelog v0.315 ([#5380](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5380)) | —(none stated). The list computes constants of the underlying type, and it is the one place those integers are written. `int(A)` would need §17.14's liftable cast lift for one position and makes `Current := Legacy` ill-typed. A ban rejects the alias and offset spellings for no safety gain. Auto-increment already makes declaration order the evaluation order, so earlier-only needs no cycle check. |
| `[EnumeratorCancellation]`-style implicit token binding in async iterators | `rejected` | spec §30.7 (*Token routing in generated async iterators*) | —(none stated). "there is no implicit binding of it to a body name (a C#-`[EnumeratorCancellation]`-style parameter binding is declined as implicit magic)." A body needing the token declares its own `CancellationToken` parameter. |
| Exact-match purity at override/implementation boundaries | `rejected` | rationale §9.7; [proposal 0042 §5, §16](0042-pure-methods.md) | —(none stated). Exact match "would sever every stdlib interface implementation from purity"; strengthening-only (never-drop / may-strengthen / monotone) is the landed rule and the delegation idiom covers the residual. |
| Expression-bodied lambdas with a non-unit body at a procedure slot (`-> ()`) — inapplicable unless the body is unit-typed, or applicable and ranked exact | `rejected` | spec §22.6 (*Lambda-literal arguments*, the procedure-slot sentence, v0.296); spec §27.3 (the discarded-result sentence, with its §21.9 discard regime); changelog v0.296 | —(none stated). §27.3's discarded-result form is a target-driven classification, so the slot admits the lambda, and the discarded result is under §21.9's regime (a warning, or the non-trivial-dispose / interface / `Task` hard error); §22.6's split already ranks a bridged body *converting*, so the discarding bridge ranks the same — exact would let `M(f: () -> ())` tie with `M(f: () -> int)` on `lambda() => 42`. |
| Extending §15.4's fresh-producer rejection beyond literal positions (to `Add`, `Insert`, and the indexer-set) | `rejected` | spec §15.4 (*Collection literals* — "deliberately **literal-only** (recorded)") | —(none stated). "A literal is a language construct whose storage semantics the compiler fully knows; it does not hide behind signature-level trust", whereas at `Add`/`Insert`/indexer-set "an opaque callee's signature type-announces no retention" — so those stay under §28.3's unbound-temporary admission and §28.11's residual-trust store rule, with the borrow-lifetime hazard backstopped by checked disposal and a mandatory warning at the fresh-unbound-temporary store position. |
| Extending §22.8's single-assignment rule to `readonly` auto-properties | `rejected` | spec §22.8, §23.1; changelog v0.271 (#3315 item 1) | A ruling that confronts what it withdraws. The reference implementation deliberately admits an initializer followed by a constructor-body store on a `readonly` auto-property, the body store overriding the initializer — established by #3207's fix and asserted twice with runtime values in `tests/module/xmod_record_body_ctor_field_store_3207_test.cpp`. `readonly` **fields** reject the same shape. Unifying them is defensible but is a ruling with its own blast radius, not a consequence of the field rule; v0.271 states the field rule and records the auto-property case as undecided. |
| Extension `static` members | `rejected` | spec §26.3 (*Restrictions* — "No `static` members") | A recorded need: "admitting extension statics would need its own resolution, merging, and ambiguity story **for no recorded need**."
