An extension block adds receiver-directed members only; statics resolve against the extended type's own declarations (§22.12), and the recorded remedy is a static method on a static class. |
| Extension type-parameter binding resolved against scope (binding only when unresolved) | `rejected` | spec §26.4 (*Generic extensions* — type-parameter binding) | —(none stated). "it would let an unrelated type declaration elsewhere silently re-mean an extension block (action at a distance)." Rationale §9.11 cites this rule as its own disqualifying precedent. |
| Folding `using var` into plain `var` | `rejected` | rationale §5.27 (v0.204) | —(none stated). Four reasons that are one: "the keyword is not an inference annotation but a declared constraint on the binding's future, and a checker cannot infer the intent it exists to check" — the borrow-pin has no substitute, the must-use lint and the keyword are one economy, the keyword is a loudness channel for API evolution, and `await using var` / `out using var` make "only `var`" unreachable anyway. The `const` half is the separate *Owned `const` locals* row. |
| Forbidding binding patterns over temporary `case` scrutinees (the reject-not-extend alternative) | `rejected` | rationale §5.24 (*The full-expression boundary and the `case`-scrutinee extension*, v0.193) | —(none stated). The §9.7-style alternative — forbid binding patterns over temporary scrutinees, as spans forbid temporary sources, and extend nothing — was "Declined, because the rejection could not be contained — to stay coherent it would have had to outlaw the expression-position form too, and that form was long-admitted and idiomatic." |
| Forcible cancellation (`Thread.Abort`-style interrupt) | `rejected` | rationale §12.4 | —(none stated). Cooperative cancellation via `CancellationToken` is the model. |
| Forcible domain teardown (Erlang's `brutal_kill`, applied to an execution domain) | `rejected` | rationale §12.4 (the ground extended to domains, v0.263); [proposal 0106 §7.3](0106-language-lessons-survey.md) | —(none stated). Ownership does **not** make asynchronous interrupt good: it improves the normal and exception paths and leaves the interrupt path as §12.4 found it, while spec §30.13's run-to-completion makes a domain a *worse* unit to kill than a thread. The need is routed to cross-domain cancellation (stdlib §6.1) and process supervision (rationale §12.14). |
| Fresh-vs-selector marker (a second ownership keyword beside `owned`) | `rejected` | rationale §5.7 (v0.243, the concrete-selector cliff); [proposal 0088](0088-concrete-static-selectors.md) | —(none stated). "it would duplicate `owned`’s class-substitution semantics keyword for keyword, differing only in value-substitution admissibility." Restricting the numeric interfaces to value conformers was declined in the same breath. |
| Full read-only restriction on a borrowed owning collection (beyond the `Dispose` bar) | `rejected` | spec §28.6 (*Borrowed owning collections*) | —(none stated). "This is narrower than a full read-only restriction, and deliberately so: Bezel's borrowed collections are already mutable (pass-and-fill is a core idiom), so the only operation a borrow-return newly makes unsound is the container `Dispose` — which is the one this rule forbids." |
| Generic `owned` fields (the `owned T` marker on a field slot) | `rejected` | rationale §10 (v0.196, #1803); rationale §5.7 (v0.196); spec §28.5 | —(none stated). Erasure lifted the marker's rejection at *signature* slots only: "the one slot that commits a type's *shape*, the generic `owned` field, deliberately stays rejected" — "a field marker commits the containing type's shape — destructor synthesis, derived properties — in a way a call-boundary marker does not." Distinct from the retired `owned T` **type-argument** modifier and from the deferred `owned`-as-generic-argument row. |
| `goto`, numeric break levels, and labels on arbitrary statements | `rejected` | rationale §17.3; spec §21.5 | —(none stated). Labeled `break`/`continue` shipped at v0.201 ([proposal 0050](0050-labeled-loops.md)) as the structured subset. |
| Function-typed record fields as borrow-carrying (widening §9.7's predicate to close §30.14's value-type hole) | `rejected` | changelog v0.266 ([#3284](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/3284)); spec §9.7, §30.14 | —(none stated) for the widening itself. Four grounds, any one sufficient: it contradicts §9.7's own stated ground (runtime-managed values have unbounded lifetime by construction, §28.9), and makes §9.7's borrow-lifetime bullets unstatable — a function-typed field names no borrow source; it over-rejects at eight consumer rules (§9.7's bullets, §30.1's async bars, §28.3's freeze, §28.11's copy slots, §22.12 rule 4, §15.3, §35.4, §30.14) to repair one; it flips a §3.6 rule-3 public-contract verdict for no gain the ruled per-component filter does not deliver; and it would still be incomplete, needing the same split cut into §15.1's and §35.4's restatements. The `ReadOnlyStringSpan` argument of #3284 is **true but not decisive** — a function-only split leaves the `string` half of the exemption intact. |
| Heap fixed-size array type (a `FixedArray<T>` beside `[N]T` and `Array<T>`) | `rejected` | rationale §5.14 (*Inline arrays and the array-kind split*) | —(none stated). "Heap arrays support fixed-size discipline by not calling resize methods"; the taxonomic simplification "keeps two array kinds (one syntactic form per kind) rather than four". |
| Implicit `ToString` in `+` concatenation | `rejected` | rationale §17.2 | —(none stated). Interpolation is the spelling. |
| Import-based audit marker for foreign-memory access — the §2.8.3 rule-2 return-type clause, and relocating `OwningBuffer<T>.DangerousPointer()` behind `System.Interop` | `rejected` | spec §2.3 and stdlib §10, §4.14 (v0.265); changelog v0.265; [#3278](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/3278) | Not new syntax, but a design that closes **all** routes at once: today three defeat any import-based marker (rule 2's use grant; the auto-imported `System`-resident FFI-boundary types; `IntPtr(longValue)` composed with `string.FromCStringPointer`), so either partial fix buys one route for a rule asymmetry (option 1) or a breaking stdlib move (option 2). Both remain available as *hardening* on their own merits — the decline is of the soundness claim, not of the hardening. |
| Information content as the failure-band test | `rejected` | rationale §11.4 (v0.229); [proposal 0076](0076-error-model-study.md) | —(none stated). The guides’ original teaching, declined as the classification test: it "breaks on the environmental fault" and would "evacuate the environmental band into `choice` returns whose exhaustive matches every interior caller would then have to write". The surviving test is *where consumption happens*. |
| Inheritance-only `when` clauses (rejecting an override that restates `when true`/`when false` — proposal 0122 L7 Option A) | `rejected` | changelog v0.288 (L7); spec §22.16, §12.1 | Evidence that the exact-match override rule (0043 Q5) is a real hazard. Option A must mint a restatement error the specification states for no clause kind, reaching every clause, with a migration over every override restating a `when` clause, for no semantic gain; Option B (one spelling, Q5 retained) has zero migration. |
| Interprocedural fork-body classification (the whole-program alternative to opaque lending by type) | `rejected` | rationale §12.9 (*Data parallelism* — the ruled lend-span design) | —(none stated). "it requires a fork-reachability classification of functions, a whole-program analysis class with no precedent here." The adopted form is opaque lending by type: a distinct lend-span family with no `Buffer` projection and `Slice`-only narrowing, so buffer recovery is closed by ordinary name resolution. |
| `is T name` binding (a pattern variable declared inside a boolean type test) | `rejected` | rationale §11.6 (v0.242; the decline predates it — spec §12.7 states it flatly) | —(none stated). Bezel splits what C#'s `is T name` bundles into three constructs: `is`/`is not` are the **boolean** type tests, with flow narrowing on locals and `readonly` fields (§7.4/§12.7) so the tested variable itself narrows and the common case needs no binding; `as` is the **nullable-binding** form; `case` typed patterns are the **multi-way binding** form. A fourth spelling would buy one line at exactly the sites narrowing does not reach (mutable fields, call results) and import C#'s recorded scoping swamp — a pattern variable declared inside a boolean expression must be scoped by definite-assignment flow, so bindings escape into `else` branches under negation (`if (not (x is T t)) return;` leaving `t` usable after), into the enclosing scope, and into `and`/`or` arms. |
| Inline fixed-size value-type array fields *(the rejection itself, retired)* | `rejected` | rationale §17.6 | **Already reopened and reversed** — rejected v0.40–v0.97, shipped v0.98 as a first-class type (spec §15.1). The entry survives in §17 as the reversal’s record and is the register’s standing precedent that a §17 row can move. |
| `KeyNotFoundException` deriving from `InvalidOperationException` | `rejected` | stdlib §4.8 (`Dictionary<K, V>`, v0.182); spec §31.4 | —(none stated). It "derives directly from `Exception` — deliberately not from `InvalidOperationException`, so that a state-fault catch arm does not silently swallow missing-key lookups". |
| `Keys`/`Values` views on `SortedDictionary<K, V>` | `rejected` | stdlib §4.17 (v0.282) | Iteration already yields entries in ascending key order, so a one-line loop substitutes. Reopening bar: a use the entry iteration cannot serve. |
| Lambda keyword alternatives (`function`, `func`/`fn`, `method`, `closure`, `proc`, `do`, `callback`) | `rejected` | rationale §3.13 (v0.217); [proposal 0064 §3](0064-keyword-lambdas.md) | The comparison is "recorded so the choice is revisitable only against these reasons". `function` was runner-up — "heaviest at exactly the hottest sites" and implies a free-function category the language lacks; `func`/`fn` would be the keyword set's first abbreviations; `method` answers "method of what?" with "nothing"; `closure` over-promises capture; `proc` is an abbreviation plus borrowed Ruby semantics; `do` would make `do (x) => do … end` legal; `callback` names a role. |
| Lambda syntax families — the literal Python form (`lambda x: body`), placeholder and implicit-parameter shorthands, brace and square blocks, sigil prefixes, and the `end`-delimited keyword form | `rejected` | rationale §3.13; [proposal 0064 §4/§4.1](0064-keyword-lambdas.md) (the §9 verification record backs every grammar claim) | —(none stated). The Python form is **colon-fatal**, verified by a failed refutation attempt: `:` is the ascription token and primitive type keywords are themselves atoms, §15.4 *defines* sequence-vs-dictionary discrimination by a top-level element colon, and §18.5's lexer cuts a hole at the format-spec colon before parsing begins. Placeholders (`_`, `it`, `$0`, `%`) are declined on *meaning* — Bezel's `_` is the discard, the exact inverse of "use this" — sigils on the words-not-sigils ground, Swift-style brace blocks on the `in`-is-membership collision, and the Elixir `fn … end` form on 0057's transferred end-noise reason. |
| Lambda-literal admission at non-call target positions under §27.1.1's value rule (an invariant value-typed return slot — no §5.1 conversion for a literal assigned to a declared function-typed location) | `rejected` | spec §27.3 (*Admission at a target-typed non-call position*, v0.292); spec §27.1.1 (closing sentence); changelog v0.292 | —(none stated). The literal is typed at the site, so the compiler bridges the conversion exactly as at a call argument (§14.5 phase 2, §22.6) and at method-reference formation (§27.1.1); the value rule governs already-formed values, which have no adapter — and it still governs an *ascribed* literal, whose written return type is a value's. |
| Lenient decode-with-replacement (U+FFFD) on the standard encodings, and big-endian UTF-16/32 variants | `rejected` | stdlib §3.2 (*`Encoding`*); changelog v0.136 | —(none stated). Strict defined-fault error model: "Silent substitution is data corruption by another name, and Bezel's design rule is that faults are defined and loud (cf. checked disposal, §28.2; serialization contract violations, §9.8). A program that wants replacement decoding writes it explicitly over the byte span." Big-endian variants declined with it — "no in-scope producer; byte-swapping is one explicit loop, and the singleton surface stays minimal." |
| Lifting of user-defined operators over `T?` (the former §7.11 deferral) | `rejected` | spec §7.11; rationale §6.4 | —(none stated). Closed as a decline at v0.267 rather than carried forward: with no built-in operator lifting, a user-declared one has nothing to be consistent with. |
| Lock poisoning (a Rust-style poisoned flag on `Locked<T>`/`RwLocked<T>`) | `rejected` | rationale §12.8 (the no-poisoning record, v0.263) | —(none stated). The interior is unaliasable (spec §30.12's scoped-guard reachability), and the class invariant re-evaluated at the next public entry (spec §22.16) is a detector with content where a poison flag carries only "someone threw". The recorded asymmetry: that detector is off in the default build mode. |
| Lock-ordering machinery, and a "no-suspend type" for guard-across-`await` | `rejected` | rationale §12.8 (*`Locked<T>`/`RwLocked<T>`* — the scope paragraph) | —(none stated). Guard-across-`await` is "a lint, not a ban (a 'no-suspend type' concept would be disproportionate)"; "lock ordering stays out of scope (the actor idiom is the recommendation when ordering gets complicated)". |
| Marked lifted operators in the F# shape (`?+?`, `?<?`) | `rejected` | rationale §6.4; [proposal 0114](0114-lifted-operators-rejected.md) | Evidence from real Bezel programs that the `??`-and-narrow spelling is materially worse — not merely longer. The design satisfies the marking principle the ruling enforces and is declined for surface cost, so it is the one recorded re-entry shape for operator-level propagation. |
| Marshal-style untyped foreign-memory surface (`Marshal.ReadInt32` over bare `IntPtr`) | `rejected` | rationale §16.3 (*Typed pointers, not an untyped `Marshal` surface*, v0.186); spec §32.12 | —(none stated). "an untyped read surface is `void*` with method-name suffixes — `Marshal.ReadDouble(p)` compiles whether `p` came from `sqlite3_column_blob` or `__errno_location`, while `Ptr<double>` vs `Ptr<int>` moves that mistake to compile time"; element-scaled `LoadAt`/`Add` also delete the hand-computed `i * sizeof(T)` bug class. The recorded cost is honest: the generic intrinsic family "is more machinery than fourteen static methods". **Verdict vocabulary is implicit** ("`Ptr<T>` won on §16.2's own argument"). |
| Marshal-style untyped foreign-memory surface (a static read/write class over bare `IntPtr` and byte offsets) | `rejected` | rationale §16.3 (*Typed pointers, not an untyped `Marshal` surface*); [proposal 0033 §11](0033-ffi-v2.md) | —(none stated). "An untyped read surface is `void*` with method-name suffixes"; element-scaled `LoadAt`/`Add` delete the hand-computed-`sizeof` bug class. The cost is recorded and borne: the generic intrinsic plus its per-instantiation gate is more machinery than fourteen static methods. |
| Member hiding | `rejected` | rationale §17.5 → §8.1 | —(none stated). |
| Member-access reflection (field/method enumeration, by-name access, dynamic instantiation) | `rejected` | rationale §5.18; §18 item *Reflection beyond `typeof`/`GetType`* (2) | —(none stated). "**declined, not deferred** … recorded as declined so the question is settled rather than perennially reopened." D-0001 and D-0002 both rest on it. |
| Moving the no-value boundary (a `Task<()>` unification, or a de-generified non-generic event family) | `rejected` | rationale §3.15 (v0.283); [proposal 0120 §4](0120-unit-type-record.md) | The boundary is frequency-shaped: the no-result task is the common case (every `async` procedure), so a mandatory `<()>` would be ambient ceremony at the highest-traffic seam; the no-payload event is its family's minority case, where the one-token instantiation is cheap and honest. Satellites follow their family head (`TaskCompletionSource` with `Task`, §30.11). Reopen on the frequency asymmetry inverting, or on a rule genuinely forking by arity in either family (none does at v0.283 — §30.5 keys on result kind, not arity). |
| Move-only parameters on `pure` members (the §22.15 v0.234 rejection, at the positions the v0.286 borrow carve reaches) | `deferred` | spec §22.15; changelog v0.286 | v0.234's ground — the argument would arrive as a value-move — no longer holds at operator operands and `Equals(T)` (they bind borrows, spec §25.2 v0.286), but the rejection stands unchanged: the carve was scoped to the comparison surface deliberately, and lifting §22.15 is its own question. Reopen on evidence a `pure` comparison surface on move-only types is wanted (a real program, per §2.8.5's bar). |
| Method and constructor parameters named `_` | `rejected` | rationale §3.2 (*Why `_` is reserved*, v0.244); [proposal 0083](0083-reserved-discard.md) | —(none stated). "Parameter names are call-site-addressable API surface (spec §22.5) and carried by the artifact interface, so an unnameable slot would be a hole in that surface; lambdas have neither concern" — which is why `lambda(_, _)` and `for var _` *were* sanctioned as true ignores in the same landing. |
| Method-reference arguments ranked other than as lambda literals — always converting (co-equal) at every callee because a reference "has no static type", or exact whenever formation succeeds | `rejected` | spec §22.6 (*Method-reference arguments*, v0.296); spec §27.6 (the mirror-direction sentence); changelog v0.296 | —(none stated). Both readings leave the typed/void pair of proposal 0002 §3 to the defaulted-arity tie-break alone and make an exact signature indistinguishable from a bridged one — the information §27.6's exact-preferred ranking already computes in the mirror direction (Appendix C.1 calls the two "mirrored"). |
| `mod` on `float` and `double` as an operator | `rejected` | spec §6.1 (*Integer arithmetic* — the `mod` domain sentence); §6.3 (*Floating-point arithmetic*) | —(none stated). "intentionally not provided as an operator": `mod` "carries no floating-point semantics in this spec — the natural choice (IEEE 754 remainder, or C `fmod`) is a deliberate decision rather than a syntactic afterthought, and naming it explicitly avoids the ambiguity." The two meanings are separately named library members, `Math.Fmod` and `Math.IEEERemainder` (stdlib §2.2). |
| Move-capturing lambdas (a closure that takes ownership of its captures) at `Domain.Spawn` | `rejected` | rationale §12.6 | —(none stated). "§28.9’s capture-is-borrow rule is a single uniform story for every closure in the language, and carving out a second capture mode for one call site would ripple into closure typing, disposal of captured-but-never-run closures, and the §28.3 escape analysis." An explicit `owned arg` parameter is semantically identical. |
| Mutable `string`-bearing and record-typed statics (admission beside the heap-free suppression set) | `rejected` | spec §22.12 (*Static members* — "declined, not deferred"); rationale §12.7 | —(none stated). A mutable string static's overwrite "forces a choice between a reclamation race … and an unbounded never-release leak"; a multi-word record static's "torn cross-domain read can fabricate a value violating the record's own invariants (§22.16)". Both stay errors, with the stdlib's synchronized spellings (`AtomicInteger<T>`, `Mutex`, `Locked<T>`) as the answer. |
| Mutable-field and property narrowing across statements | `rejected` | rationale §6.1; spec §7.4 | —(none stated). "Bezel declines to narrow mutable fields and properties entirely"; `readonly` fields narrow since v0.214. |
| Naked-identifier bindings in named-destructure slots (`Rect(Width = w)` binding `w`, the F#/OCaml reading) | `rejected` | rationale §3.14; [proposal 0065 §3.4, §2.3](0065-named-destructure-patterns.md) | —(none stated); 0065's own status fixes "the settled forks (§3) are not reopened". Directly after the equality operator a silent binding inverts the interrogation reading, and a name that happens to resolve to a `const` would silently flip a binding into an equality test as constants are added. The positional naked-binding shorthand survives unchanged where it is unambiguous; bindings in named slots are spelled `var w`. |
| Named **uncatchable** `OutOfMemoryException` (report symmetry with `StackOverflowException`) | `rejected` | rationale §4.4 (the explicit `Declined:` item) | "the name is cosmetic — and **revisitable without breaking anything**." A distinct, and far lower, bar than the catchable question above: the report’s wording is implementation territory, so nothing depends on the name. |
| Named-argument spellings for `ref`, `out`, and `scoped` arguments (the §22.5 halves left positional-only at v0.260) | `rejected` | spec §22.5; Appendix A.4; [proposal 0102 §1.3](0102-small-records.md); changelog v0.260 | —(none stated). The transfer-marked half lifted at v0.260 — a marker is part of the argument *expression*, not an `ArgModifiers` prefix — while these three "stay positional-only because they are **true argument modifiers**: grammar-level prefixes on the argument, not parts of its expression". For `out`'s binding forms a named spelling would put `:=` in two roles on one argument (`M(result := out var x)`, `M(out var result := n)`), the collision A.4 records as having "no reading"; `scoped` shares the alternative and therefore the shape (§22.3's lending-mode line). Argued in 0102 §1.3 and carried into §22.5's own text rather than left silent — the shape of the `out owned`/`scoped` overload-ban row above. |
| Named arguments at a call through a function-typed value — binding *by* name (the §27.1 names as caller-bindable surface) | `rejected` | spec §27.1 (v0.295); changelog v0.295; [#4085](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4085) | —(none stated). The names a function type declares are documentation and not part of type identity (§27.1: the named and unnamed forms are the same type), so an ordinary assignment — exact-match, or §27.1.1's identity-preserving retyping — may drop or relabel them, so a binding keyed on them would mean one thing through one view of a value and another through the next; and a function type declares no defaults, so §22.5's named-binding machinery has nothing to do that position does not (§23.5's reasoning at the index position). The named form is rejected outright at such a call. |
| Named arguments at a call through a function-typed value — order-checked *labels* (a written name admitted only where it equals the declared name at that position) | `rejected` | spec §27.1 (v0.295); changelog v0.295; [#4085](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4085) | A revision that makes function-type parameter names part of type identity (§27.1). Until then a label is a contract on a name the type does not carry: a retyping to the unnamed form (§27.1.1), or a rename in the function type, would break every call site that spelled it — documentation-only names must stay free to change. |
| Named-destructure transfer — unmarked symmetry, and the receiver-side marker spellings | `rejected` | rationale §3.14 (the v0.250 resolution); [proposal 0092 §7](0092-named-destructure-transfer.md) | —(none stated). **Unmarked symmetry** (named slots transferring exactly as positional ones do) "deletes the named form's ability to borrow one payload while transferring another in the same list, the one capability no other form supplies" — the decline "rests on the per-slot peek alone", and the record concedes the comparison was closer than first presented. The **receiver-side spellings** fall on operand identity: `Field = move x` / `move var x` make `move`'s operand a fresh name, and `Field = owned var x` reintroduces `owned` at a site performing an action. `move N = _` stays inadmissible — it would mint a new consuming site K3 does not enumerate. |
| `not in` as a two-token membership operator | `rejected` | spec §17.5 (*The `in` operator*) | —(none stated). "`in` desugars to a plain `bool`-returning `Contains` call, so negation composes with nothing lost but one pair of parentheses, and admitting `x not in c` would only half-serve the habit it targets — `not x in c` would still parse the other way." `is not` earns its two-token spelling because it negates a *narrowing* operator; membership has no such semantics to protect. |
| `not` as a user-overloadable operator | `rejected` | rationale §14.4 | —(none stated). `~` remains overloadable; logical-style negation takes a named method. |
| Offset parameters on the span byte accessors (offset-as-argument instead of slice-then-read) | `rejected` | stdlib §4.14 (*No offset parameter*) | —(none stated). "The alternative (offset as a parameter) would split the API into two access patterns and add one parameter per method, both costs the slice-then-read form avoids"; `Slice` is a record construction with no allocation. Distinct from the bit tier's advance-shape row (which is about cursor advance, not read offset). **Verdict vocabulary is implicit.** |
| `old(E)` over a move-only operand — capture-by-borrow, and element-wise snapshot | `rejected` | rationale §9.8; spec §22.16 (v0.253, spec defect #3113) | —(none stated). Borrow capture "would observe **post-state** (an `old` that is silently not old)"; the snapshot is "disproportionate". The adopted arm rejects the operand and names the copyable-projection remedy. |
| One interface carrying both collection-literal forms (two statics, or a carrier type parameter) | `rejected` | [proposal 0125 §2.10](0125-static-interface-promotions.md); [proposal 0115 §3.3](0115-collection-literal-borrowing-span-family.md) (its bar, now met and kept closed) | —(none stated). Abstract-only statics force every conformer to supply both; contradicts *exactly one form per type*. |
| Operator pointer arithmetic (`p + 1`, `-`, `<`) and `Ptr<Ptr<T>>` | `rejected` | rationale §16.3 | —(none stated). "nothing in the binding corpus (sqlite3, curl, BLAS, POSIX) needs them." |
| Ordering operators (`<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`) on `DateTime`, `TimeSpan`, `DateOnly` and `Guid` | `rejected` | stdlib §2.5, §2.9; changelog v0.274 ruling 1 | A stdlib pass that takes the surface on its own merits. Four comments asserted the operator group existed and agreed with `CompareTo`; none of the four operators is declared anywhere, and records auto-generate `=`/`<>`/`GetHashCode`/`ToString` but never ordering — so `deadline < DateTime.UtcNow` did not compile. Declaring them is a surface addition carrying §25.2's whole-group and co-declaration obligations; `CompareTo` already serves ordering, so v0.274 deleted the false claim instead. |
| `out owned` and `scoped` halves of the §22.6 overload ban, left un-lifted at v0.250 | `rejected` | rationale §3.10; [proposal 0091 §3](0091-owned-overload-lift.md) | —(none stated). The ordinary-parameter half lifted at v0.250; these two halves "deliberately **stand**", argued in 0091 §3 rather than left silent. |
| `=` overloading on classes | `rejected` | rationale §7.2 | —(none stated). Classes customize equality through `Equals`; records and enums may overload `=`/`<>` freely. |
| Overload-aware relational and range patterns: reading a scrutinee type's declared `operator <` group, or the `<` a constraint grants a type parameter, as the ordering a §19.3 pattern tests | `rejected` | spec §19.3, §16.6 (v0.314); rationale §11.11; changelog v0.314 ([#5269](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5269)) | A program whose ordering test a guard (`var x when x > limit`) cannot express, or a `Range<T>` element widening that admits user element types, at which point both pattern forms move together. A declared group is four methods §25.2 obliges only to coexist, a type may declare several heterogeneous groups, and the range form is bound to §16.3's intrinsic set by the deferred widening. |
| Overload-resolution ranking of built-in implicit conversions (C#’s better-conversion-target rule) | `rejected` | rationale §14.5 | —(none stated). Ranking would be net-new semantics over spec §5.1, not a clarification of it; the ambiguity error stands. |
| Owned `const` locals (the permissive owned-`const`) | `rejected` | rationale §17.8; §5.27 | —(none stated). Declined as an undeclared-disposal duplicate of `using var`. |
| Owned keys in `OwningDictionary` (`OwningDictionary<owned K, V>`) | `rejected` | stdlib §4.9 | —(none stated). "`OwningDictionary` does **not** support owned keys … Realistic key types are value-like (string, int, records, etc.); cases for owned class-typed keys are rare to nonexistent in practice." |
| `owned` marker on fresh-task-returning signatures | `rejected` | rationale §12.10 (*Task ownership at the call site: calls mint, storage lends*, v0.190; re-declined v0.258) | —(none stated). "The blast radius alone was disqualifying — every async signature in every program, every task-returning function type", and the unmarked form would then mean either a leak-by-omission borrow (worst through interfaces) or force the marker everywhere — §5.5's "redundant noise" verdict transplanted. Re-declined verbatim at v0.258 when the marker was proposed as the iterable transplant: "it would churn every static iterable declaration and stdlib operator to buy an announcement the declared type already carries, and reinstate the leak wherever omitted." |
| Panama-style checked foreign memory (bounds/lifetime-carrying handles) | `rejected` | rationale §16.3 | —(none stated). "the checking would be exactly as good as the trusted declarations it derives from"; `RawBytes.FromForeign(p, length)` *is* the length-carrying form, "and a general checked tier would promise verification §32’s trust posture cannot deliver". |
| `parallel for` statement, and user-generic parallel libraries | `rejected` | rationale §12.9 (*Data parallelism: MOVE, FREEZE, and the recorded fork-join design*, v0.186) | For user-generic parallel libraries the record states an **enabling path**, not a bar: "the compiler-known table can widen later without breaking". For `parallel for` no bar is stated: "its safety needs per-index write-disjointness dependence analysis the spec could neither state simply nor check totally; a runtime check hides per-iteration cost, UB is not in the vocabulary, and a 'trust me' independence annotation is exactly what §3.6 rule 1 forbids." |
| `params` / variadic parameters | `rejected` | rationale §17.9; spec §22.14 | —(none stated). The collection-literal call site keeps the allocation visible. |
| Per-site suppression spellings for the five statically-certain-hazard lints (fresh-store, terminal-accessor, mutation-during-iteration, element-borrow use-after-removal) | `rejected` | rationale §5.28 (*Silent tracking* — the three lints; the fourth and fifth instances, v0.307); [proposal 0129 §4 item 9](0129-iteration-mutation-lints.md) | —(none stated). "**No per-site suppression spelling exists for any of the three lints**" (now five), deliberately: the remedies — bind to a named holder; materialize or take the owning path — are one line each, already sanctioned, and convert the program from hazardous to sound, whereas a suppression spelling would be a new way to write the silent dangle the lints exist to remove"; project-level lint tuning stays the only knob. |
| Pooling the two members' constraint sets in §22.6's declaration guard, so that a generic `owned`/plain pair's position qualifies when either member's constraints exclude the collapsing substitutions (`M<T>(x: T) where T: SomeClass` beside an unconstrained `M<T>(owned x: T)` admitted) | `rejected` | spec §22.6, §11.3, Appendix C.1 (v0.316); rationale §10; changelog v0.316 ([#5379](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5379)) | Constraints do not make a candidate inapplicable (spec §14.5), so a substitution one member excludes still reaches both candidates: `M(5)`, which the unconstrained member alone accepts, would collapse into an ambiguity. Reopening bar: constraint-filtered applicability (its own row) being lifted. |
| Post-substitution keying of ownership classification | `rejected` | rationale §5.7 (the v0.194 declines); rationale §12.10 (v0.190; re-declined v0.258) | —(none stated). Classifying each call by its *substituted* return type "reads plausibly at `Math.Max<Connection>` and is refuted one shelf over: `First<T>(): T` at `T = Task<int>` would substitute into the `Task`-family rule and mint a disposal obligation over a collection-owned element." The sound geometry is pre-substitution keying with per-instantiation enforcement. Declined independently at v0.190 and v0.194 and re-declined at v0.258. |
| Primary-wins exception supersession (the Java try-with-resources flip) | `rejected` | rationale §11.8 (v0.259); changelog v0.259; [proposal 0097](0097-superseded-exception-snapshots.md) | "field evidence from external users that upstream `catch` arms are matching on cleanup failures often enough to matter — arm matching on the primary is the one gain the snapshot does not provide." The snapshot "does not foreclose it": a future primary-wins would keep the same chain. |
| Process-global owning-choice singleton (a `static readonly` field or auto-property of an owning-classified choice) | `rejected` | spec §22.12 rule 4's residual (v0.265); changelog v0.265; [#3273](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/3273) | A stated exit-disposal enrolment for owning-choice-typed static fields, symmetric to §22.13's owning-inline-array clause — i.e. a §28-family disposal-site addition with its kernel classification, not a re-reading of §22.12. The workaround (a `static owned readonly` class field holding the choice, §15.3) is one line, which is why the rejection is cheap. |
| Publication-confinement alternatives for `CancellationToken.Register` — a stated programmer obligation, cross-domain callback queueing, and a static rejection | `rejected` | stdlib §6.1 (*The alternatives, declined with reasons*) | The static-rejection arm carries an explicit ground-of-decline: "It is declined on that cost, not on principle" — the analysis "would be a whole-program value-provenance analysis over token values with no existing carrier to ride", where every other per-value analysis in Bezel is method-local. Leaving confinement to the programmer is refuted by two of the four paths (last-handle drop, transitive linked firing) not being visible at the site that would honour it; queueing a foreign callback to the registering domain "would break the guarantee `Register` exists to give" — §28.14 quiescence. Also recorded there: the lexical layer of §30.12's two-layer shape is "deliberately **not** mirrored", publication being a property of the receiver's history rather than the call site's syntax. |
| `pure` on §4.5's `Capacity` property | `rejected` | stdlib §4.5; spec §22.15; changelog v0.274 ruling 6; [#3324](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/3324) | A ruling on whether `OwningBuffer<T>.Length` is pure. Omitting `pure` is a compile error under §22.15 dispatch rule 1; declaring it is *also* rejected, because the body calls the non-pure `Length` — §22.15 rule 5's own worked example. v0.274 added `pure` to the sibling `Count` (whose body reads a field) and left `Capacity` alone rather than introduce a fresh contradiction while fixing one. |
| Readonly bare-`T` statics classified **at the declaration** — blanket admission | `rejected` | spec §22.12 rule 4; changelog v0.262 (#3269) | —(none stated). "blanket admission ignores the class-typed substitutions rule 4 exists to warn on." |
| Readonly bare-`T` statics classified **at the declaration** — blanket rejection | `rejected` | spec §22.12 rule 4; changelog v0.262 (#3269) | —(none stated). "blanket rejection would ban `static readonly` of `T` at every immutable substitution for the sake of the ones the warning already covers." |
| Readonly local bindings (`let`, `val`, `readonly var`) | `rejected` | rationale §17.8 | —(none stated) for a fourth local form. The named candidate landed in another shape: runtime `const` (v0.201, [proposal 0049](0049-runtime-const-and-deferred-adoptions.md) train A) is the endorsed readonly-local idiom. |
| Receiver sameness by a record's own `Equals` in function-value equality | `rejected` | spec §27.7.1 (*Equality of function values* — the value-shaped-receiver bullet); changelog v0.180 | —(none stated). It "would drag user equality dispatch into function-value comparison and make `-=` matching sensitive to value mutation between subscribe and unsubscribe." The consequence — deterministic `-=` for record-receiver method references not expressible in v1 — is carried instead, with `Subscribe` plus the owned `Subscription` as the remedy and §28.14 bound 3 marked liftable. |
| Recursive-instantiation rule precision — the three recorded over-rejections | `rejected` | spec §14.6; changelog v0.262; [proposal 0104 §4.3](0104-witness-abi-open-questions.md) | —(none stated). The per-edge test is "deliberately stronger than closure-boundedness, and decidable edge-by-edge at the declaration"; the three costs (dynamically-unreachable growing edges, in-SCC `F<T?>`, closed-edge-bounded mixed cycles) are recorded, not hidden. |
| Removing binary bitwise operator overloadability (`&`, `\|`, `^`, `<<`, `>>`) | `rejected` | rationale §14.4 (the v0.284 keep paragraph); [proposal 0121 §2](0121-subtraction-batch-2026-08.md) | Grammar-touching (Appendix A's shared `OpToken`/`IfaceStaticOperator` productions), strands the compound-assignment desugar, and leaves §14.4's `~` set-complement ground incoherent; 0078's investigation "found **no flags-enum idiom exists** in the language or library" (its recorded flags idiom is const integer masks, needing no overloadability), so no current client is foreclosed-in-waiting; the keep is structural. Record ≈ one paragraph; removal ≈ a design-change revision. Re-measured each subtraction pass. **Re-measured at v0.289** (proposal 0122 L4): `++`/`--` left the overloadable set — the `OpToken` cost was paid there, and the other two grounds (the compound-assignment desugar; §14.4's `~` set-complement argument) do not reach `++`/`--` — so this keep stands unchanged, on its own three grounds. |
| Removing the sixty per-type `Write`/`WriteLine`/`Append` rows on `Console`, `StringBuilder` and `TextWriter` (keeping the `Object` overloads) | `rejected` | stdlib §2.1 (the unboxed-lowering sentence, v0.290); [proposal 0123 §5 item 3](0123-stdlib-regularization-batch-2026-09.md); [proposal 0074 §7](0074-stdlib-ergonomics-batch-3.md) | Stdlib §1's performance disclaimer makes signatures normative and characteristics not — it does not forbid performance-motivated surface (read that way it would condemn `TryWrite*` and `File.WriteAllBytes(path, ReadOnlySpan<byte>)` too); two adjacent 0074 declines (`Console.ErrorLine`, `Append(Object)`) take the family as load-bearing; the lane's proposed keep-set named a `Console.Write(char)` that does not exist. The additive half — an `Object`-overload call with a primitive argument lowers without boxing — is recorded at §2.1. Reopening bar: —(none stated). |
| Removing the unit type in favor of a C#-style `void` (a non-type no-result marker in function types) | `rejected` | rationale §3.15 (v0.283); [proposal 0120 §3–§4](0120-unit-type-record.md) | The member-bifurcation cost: with no type instantiating `T` for a no-result operation, `method Retry<T>(op: () -> T): T` and every result-generic combinator needs a no-result twin — structural function types escape C#'s `Action`/`Func` naming split but only a unit type escapes the member split. Unit is invisible in ordinary code already (§22.1's procedure form). Reopen on evidence the uniformity fails in practice: a real higher-order surface unit-instantiation cannot serve, or measured confusion from `()` the procedure form does not hide. |
| Redacting non-public state from carried contract-clause text | `rejected` | rationale §9.9 | —(none stated). "a redacted record cannot be agreement-checked or diffed, which forfeits the staleness backstop." |
| Redundant use-site transfer markers at announced targets | `rejected` | rationale §3.10; record: [proposal 0102 §2](0102-small-records.md) | —(none stated) for the use side. Explicitly **does not foreclose the reverse direction** — tightening the declaration side by rejecting redundant `owned` — which is a separate question with its own costs. |
| Refcounting arbitrary classes by annotation; compiler-inferred sharing by escape analysis | `rejected` | rationale §5.21 | —(none stated). "hides cost, breaks the structural-facts discipline"; "implicit magic; the language’s position is that ownership transitions are visible in source." |
| Rejecting opaque function-typed parameter arguments at deferred-activation calls | `rejected` | rationale §5.25 (*Callbacks that outlive the call*, v0.196) | —(none stated). "Rejecting opaque parameter arguments instead was declined as outlawing half the motivation" — a plain function-typed parameter of the enclosing body contributes *itself* as a carried source, which is what keeps the forwarding combinator (`Filtered(xs, f) => xs.Where(f)`) compositional. |
| Relational and range patterns over a nullable scrutinee (retaining the never-matches reading) | `rejected` | spec §19.3, §16.6; rationale §6.4 | —(none stated). Retaining it would reproduce in pattern position the split verdict v0.267 removes, and would leave §16.6's "agrees exactly with `in`" false — `in` requires a non-nullable left operand (spec §17.5). Constant patterns over a nullable scrutinee are unaffected: equality over an absent value has an answer, ordering does not. |
| Relaxed and acquire-release atomic memory orderings | `rejected` | stdlib §6.4 (*`Atomic<T>`: load, store, exchange, compare-and-swap*); changelog v0.140 | "the surface stays minimal until profiles demand otherwise." All atomics are sequentially consistent, participating in a single global order; the weaker orderings "were considered and declined". |
| Renaming the lazy `Reverse()` operator to `Reversed()` | `rejected` | stdlib §5 (*`Reverse` (naming note, v0.195)*) | —(none stated). §5 keeps the .NET-heritage operator names, so the lazy `Reverse()` "deliberately sits outside §4.4's imperative-mutates/participle-copies convention — recorded as a decision, not an accident (renaming to `Reversed()` was declined: §4.4's participle promises a fresh owned *copy*, which a lazy sequence is not)." |
| Renaming §17.1's precedence level 10 ("Relational") to disambiguate Appendix C.1's operator list | `rejected` | spec Appendix C.1, §17.1, §17.2; changelog v0.271 (#3315 item 4) | Evidence that the parenthetical is insufficient. The rename would touch thirteen band-sense sites including three grammar productions (`Relational`/`RelTail`/`RelOp`), §17.4 and Appendix B.2, and would **not** eliminate the two-sense usage, since §17.2 keeps it. §17.2's own spelling — *relational (`<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`)* — is the house precedent the parenthetical follows, and it costs one clause instead of thirteen edits. |
| `require else` / `ensure then` precondition-refinement syntax | `rejected` | rationale §9.8; spec §22.16 behavioral-subtyping rule 3 | "declined until demand materializes." Overrides inherit the precondition verbatim; no weakening syntax exists in this revision. |
| Reserved-`_` alternatives — recording the identifier reading instead, C#'s contextual discard, deleting `using var _`, and reserving every `_`-leading identifier | `rejected` | rationale §3.2 (*Why `_` is reserved*, v0.244); [proposal 0083 §6](0083-reserved-discard.md) | —(none stated). Recording the corner was declined because rationale §3.2 already *disowns* it and the costs keep accruing at every future modifier; C#'s contextual rule "is approximately today's rule, and is the source of the split-brain", kept in C# only for backward compatibility Bezel does not have; deleting `using var _` was declined because the anonymous scope-end hold is corpus-proven (72 sites) and is discard-*shaped*; reserving every `_`-leading identifier "breaks the `_camelCase` convention and thousands of corpus sites for no semantic gain" — only the bare token is reserved. |
| Restricting the §15.4.1 collection-literal protocol at class-typed `T` | `rejected` | spec §15.4.1, §28.11; changelog v0.270 (#3310, routed to a proposal) | Superseded rather than barred: the defect is that the borrowing form names the *owning*-family view (`ReadOnlySpan<T>`, backed by `OwningBuffer<T>`) for a protocol whose elements are defined as borrows. Restricting at class `T` is self-defeating — §28.11 places borrow slots *only* in the class/interface row, so the restriction would forbid the form precisely where its own definition places it. Closing it is a source-breaking signature change and takes the proposal path (spec-change-process §6). |
| `Result<T, E>` extractors — `Unwrap` and a lazy `UnwrapOrElse` | `rejected` | stdlib §2.20 (`Result<T, E>`, v0.200) | —(none stated). "There is no `Unwrap` — a method receives `this` as a borrow (spec §35.2), so an owned-payload extractor is unspellable as a method, correctly: extraction is consumption, and consumption is the owning match's job, exhaustiveness-checked where `Unwrap` would fault." `UnwrapOr(fallback)` takes an ordinary value argument evaluated at the call site: "there is no lazy `UnwrapOrElse`". |
| `Result`-first error model (migrating exceptions to `Result<T, E>`) | `rejected` | rationale §11.4 (v0.229); [proposal 0076](0076-error-model-study.md) | —(none stated). The maximal question was asked directly and "landed as doctrine — the failure-signal rule (stdlib §1) — rather than migration". |
| Retained-thunk minting (the `GetFunctionPointerForDelegate` shape) | `rejected` | rationale §16.4 | —(none stated). "precisely a retained environment with no bounding frame." |
| Runtime null fault at an operator with an absent operand | `rejected` | rationale §6.4; [proposal 0114](0114-lifted-operators-rejected.md) | —(none stated). Declined for coherence, not cost: it needs no new exception type (`NullReferenceException`, spec §31.4), but a faulting operator can never yield `null`, so it is the adopted rejection with the diagnosis moved to production — against Appendix C.1, spec §7.8's explicit `!`, and the §5.4/§6.5 pattern of faulting only where static rejection is unavailable. |
| `scoped` and `ctx` as the extern callback-environment marker, and marker-free slot inference | `rejected` | rationale §16.4 (*Why `env` and not `scoped`*, v0.186); spec §32.8 | —(none stated). Three grounds against `scoped`: it "would give one keyword opposite meanings" (in-language `scoped` is the storage-bounded tier, the extern clause the call-bounded one); v0.175's "`scoped` is illegal on extern" reasoning "remains true and is kept, not reversed"; and the marker's grammatical job is ABI slot designation, not a lifetime tier. `ctx` "lost to `env` (what Bezel puts there — the closure environment)". Slot inference with no marker "is impossible": `sqlite3_exec` has two pointer-shaped candidate slots and the callback-side position genuinely varies across libraries. |
| `scoped` as the spelling for iterator-frame retention; and leaving that retention documented-but-unverified | `rejected` | rationale §5.25 | —(none stated). The documented-but-unverified posture "was declined more firmly than anywhere else it has come up" — v0.179 had already rejected it for async. |
| Scope-first-with-case-fallback resolution for declared-choice results | `rejected` | rationale §9.11 (v0.233) | —(none stated). "declined as action at a distance: under it, adding a helper method (or local) anywhere in scope would silently re-mean a `return` statement far away." The authoritative rule plus a shadowing warning was taken instead. |
| `scoped`-dropping variance at interface implementation matching (the semantically safe direction) | `rejected` | spec §11.2 (*`scoped` in interface signatures*) | —(none stated). Dropping the interface's `scoped` "is the safe direction semantically (the callee retains less than announced), but it is rejected all the same": `scoped` is part of a function type's identity (§27.1) and never distinguishes overloads (§22.6), "so the matching rule that keeps signatures exact elsewhere keeps it exact here — any variance would be a third, position-local rule with no compensating capability". Distinct from the *unsound* tightening direction, which is a soundness rule, not a decline. |
| Semantic normalization (commutativity, folding, SMT implication) of clause text for interface identity | `rejected` | rationale §9.9 | —(none stated). It "would make the classification depend on prover strength." |
| Sendability requirement on throwable types, and runtime graph-checking of exception objects | `rejected` | rationale §12.6 | —(none stated). One question (why faults cross as `DomainFaultedException`), two declined mechanisms: a sendability requirement on throwables is "intrusive, retroactive"; runtime graph-checking pays the runtime-check cost "on the unhappy path". |
| Send/Sync-style thread-safety typing (with its two rival postures: document-the-status-quo; locks-as-discipline) | `rejected` | rationale §12.5; §18 (*`sendable` as a `where` constraint*) | —(none stated). The opt-in `sendable` `where` constraint shipped instead at v0.201; the words "stands untouched" sit in §18’s entry, recording that §12.5’s decline of inference-driven Send/Sync typing survives it. |
| Serialization member-name mechanisms — attributes, Swift `CodingKeys`, and a type-level `serialized tolerant` clause | `rejected` | rationale §5.19 (*Serialization without reflection*) | —(none stated). *Attributes* were rejected because field-name customization "is precisely the canonical case that, in C#, requires reflection"; *`CodingKeys`* "is a footgun by construction — it conflates which members are coded with what they are renamed to into one exhaustive list"; the type-level `serialized tolerant` clause fell on a category distinction — the v0.117 effects are intrinsic to the type, tolerance is a property of one read. |
| `SharedEventSource<T>` as a `Shared<T>` payload, and its displacement-unbounded classification | `rejected` | stdlib §6.5 (*Events* — the Statics/Facts bullets, v0.187); spec §28.13, §22.12 rule 2 | —(none stated). It is "deliberately not in spec §28.13's `Shared`-payload set (`Shared<SharedEventSource<T>>` would add a second distribution mechanism — the self-distributing fact)"; static reachability, not transfer, is its distribution mechanism. Separately, "*Displacement-unbounded* was considered and declined: a displaced source leaks with its subscriber list intact, an accepted cost — mutable statics of the type stay admitted." |
| Sharing as a third reference kind beside owned and borrowed | `rejected` | rationale §5.21 | —(none stated). "it would have touched every join rule, every producer table entry, and every feature that dispatches on ownership state, for no expressiveness the handle form lacks." |
| Silent tracking — alt. 1: the layered runtime (escape analysis → arenas → domain-local RC → atomic promotion) | `rejected` | rationale §5.28 (*Alternatives considered and declined*, item 1) | —(none stated). "every layer past the first two would have to be built from nothing … a hidden per-type runtime purchased for a feature whose value is ceremony." |
| Silent tracking — alt. 2: restricting trivial-dispose to provably acyclic types | `rejected` | rationale §5.28 (item 2) | —(none stated). "Declined for non-locality: acyclicity is a property of the global type-reference graph." It would also break spec §3.6’s basis that a derived property is computed from the type’s own *structure*. |
| Silent tracking — alt. 3: uniform loud ceremony (all classes tracked *and* warned) | `rejected` | rationale §5.28 (item 3) | —(none stated). "Declined by the recorded v0.61→v0.66 history: the warning had no content then and has none now." |
| Silent tracking — alt. 4: a declared opt-out marker (or the empty-destructor idiom) | `rejected` | rationale §5.28 (item 4) | —(none stated). "Solves nothing structural … Subsumed: under silent tracking there is nothing to opt out of." |
| Silent tracking — alt. 5: uniform silence (Rust-style implicit drop for everything) | `rejected` | rationale §5.28 (item 5, added v0.239 when the owner posed it directly) | —(none stated). Ceremony is "the **announcement of observable disposal effects**", so the criterion — disposal provably runs no user code — is "already maximal: extending silence past it means announcing effects nowhere." |
| `Single` as a second PascalCase alias for `float` (documenting the undocumented resolver arm) | `rejected` | changelog v0.288 (#3812); spec §3.4; stdlib §9.2 | —(none stated). A newly minted duplicate spelling against the stdlib duplicate-spelling row's posture — "tolerate inherited duplicates, mint no new ones" — that would also have to enlarge §3.4's reserved set; the arm is deleted instead. |
| Slice-asymmetry repair confined to the bit tier (an eager `Slice` beside the byte tier's lazy one) | `rejected` | stdlib §4.14 (*Bit-granular accessors* — the `Slice` note) | —(none stated). "The asymmetry is inherited deliberately rather than repaired here: repairing it in the bit tier alone would make one tier's `Slice` eager and the neighbouring tier's lazy under the same name — precisely the divergence a mirrored surface exists to prevent — and the lazy shape is the one this tier's own totality convention already assumes." |
| Source constructor on `OwningLinkedList<T>` (a populate-from-source form on the owning linked list) | `rejected` | stdlib §4.7 (*There is no source constructor*); changelog v0.265 | —(none stated) for the constructor itself: a borrowing source owns nothing to give (stdlib §4.1) and an owning source is drained, not copied — the `OwningArray<T>.ToArray()` ground (stdlib §4.5). Separately open, and **not** covered by this row: whether the owning linked list should gain the bulk owned-ingress *method* `OwningArray<T>` and `OwningSet<T>` already have (`AddRange(owned …)`, `UnionWith(owned …)`) — a stdlib addition, which takes its own proposal. |
| Stack-only spans (C#'s `ref struct` `Span<T>`) | `rejected` | stdlib §4.14 (*Why not stack-allocated like C#'s `Span<T>`?*); spec §30.1 | —(none stated). C#'s stack-only `ref struct` "gives it the strongest possible lifetime guarantees but constrains where it can appear (no `Span<T>` fields in classes, no `Span<T>` in async state machines, no `Span<T>` returned across await points)". Bezel's span is an ordinary borrow-bearing record: "The Bezel choice trades the unconditional stack-only safety of C# for a much smaller language footprint — no new 'ref struct' type kind is introduced", with the borrow tracker rejecting programs whose buffer lifetimes cannot be proven. **Verdict vocabulary is implicit.** |
| Static dispatch through an interface-typed value | `rejected` | spec §11.8 (*Static interface members* — *Static-member interfaces as ordinary types*, consequence 1) | —(none stated). "a value's static type names no single conforming type, so 'static dispatch through a value' would be hidden dynamic dispatch, which Bezel declines." `T.Member(...)` dispatch requires a generic type **parameter** constrained by the interface; calling a static member on the interface *name* (`INumber<int>.Zero()`) is a compile error — the interface declares no bodies to run. |
| Static-event alternatives S2 and S3 (regressing the capture-free guarantee; silently synchronizing instance events) | `rejected` | rationale §13.6 (*Retiring the `event` member kind*, v0.187 — Statics (S1)); [proposal 0035](0035-event-member-demotion.md); changelog v0.187 | —(none stated). S2 would regress the capture-free guarantee the sealed `ISharedEvent<out T>`/`SharedEventSource<T>` pair exists to make enforceable (the first enforceable statement of the v0.163/v0.180 rule-5 guarantee; #1647 closes by construction); S3 would silently synchronize instance events, "reversing v0.163's recorded instance-events-unsynchronized choice". |
| Static-interface `Self` type (a self-type keyword for the recurring conformer) | `rejected` | spec §11.8 (*Static interface members*) | —(none stated) for v1: "no new syntax, no `Self` keyword (deliberately declined for v1 — the recurrence is explicit and already expressible)" through the ordinary constraint machinery (`class Meters: INumber<Meters>`). Static dispatch through a *value* is declined in the same record as "hidden dynamic dispatch". |
| Statics-and-sharing taxonomy alternatives — the three-list status quo, deriving only §28.13, a user-declarable trait, and facts-in-tooling-only | `rejected` | rationale §12.13 (*The statics-and-sharing taxonomy: facts, not lists*, v0.245); [proposal 0087](0087-statics-vocabulary.md) | —(none stated). One question (how statics-and-sharing membership is presented), four declined answers: the status quo leaks checker taxonomy into the user's mental model ("one `static` field could meet a hard error, two co-firing warnings, or silence depending on which of three lists its type sat in"); deriving only §28.13 is "most of the cost, half the benefit"; a user-declarable trait misreads the facts, which are "compiler-known *trust grants* about specific implementations … not self-certifiable structural properties" — opening them to user types "is a real language design, named so it is not smuggled in as notation"; and facts-in-tooling-only leaves "two normative statements of one truth", the drift class `check_docs.sh` exists to catch. |
| Strict no-carve-out throw rule for pure bodies | `rejected` | rationale §9.7; [proposal 0042 §4.5, §16](0042-pure-methods.md) | —(none stated). Without the carve-out (a trivial-dispose class with a pure constructor as the immediate throw operand) "argument validation would be unwritable in the whole pure fragment and programmers would launder faults through provoked built-ins". |
| Structural / anonymous unions (`A \| B`) | `rejected` | rationale §9.6; §18 sum-types entry | —(none stated). "Anti-nominal … a direct collision with the recorded nominal-products posture (D-0002)." |
| Structural/implicit conformance to `ICollectionLiteralTarget` (the interface as an internal predicate; the factory alone as the opt-in) | `rejected` | spec §15.4.1 (*Recognition category*); rationale §5.16; [proposal 0125 §2.6](0125-static-interface-promotions.md) | —(none stated). A second structural rule beside §17.5's `Contains`; buys prose only. |
| Subclass-must-be-sendable inheritance rules | `rejected` | rationale §12.6 | —(none stated). "it inverts the dependency — a base author’s coincidental sendability would constrain every subclass author, violating the principle that derived type properties are observations, not obligations." |
| Synchronous-access, async-initialized plain class-typed statics (the write-once-before-first-use form) | `rejected` | spec §22.13 (*Static initialization order* — pattern (a)) | —(none stated). "There is deliberately **no** synchronous-access, async-initialized, plain class-typed static: rule 2 admits no such form, and a write-once-before-first-use form would not be checkable at the declaration (rule 2's admission is keyed to the declared type so it stays decidable)." The recorded shapes are eager startup assignment and the lazy async accessor that caches the *task*. |
| Syntactic reading of §31.1's *captured by a closure that may outlive the arm* — rejecting every closure over a catch binding that is returned from any lambda in the body (the #3546 pre-pass taken as the rule), a blanket rejection of every closure over the binding, or a fourth admitted capture form (a closure bound to a *plain* local inside the arm) stated in §31.1 alone | `rejected` | spec §31.1 (*Which closures may outlive the arm*, v0.297); spec §28.3 (the closed three-form closure-capture list through v0.297; the source-set rule since v0.298, [proposal 0126](0126-closure-borrow-source-set.md)), §28.9, §28.14; [#3569](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/3569) (open: the implementation's narrowing); changelog v0.297 | —(none stated) for the first two: neither is §28.3's closure-capture rule, which keys on the closure's value escaping its source's scope, not on where a `return` keyword sits. The plain-local form was declined *here* — as a §31.1-only statement — because it would have widened §28.3's then-closed list from one section; that widening was then taken by the §6 route this cell named: [proposal 0126](0126-closure-borrow-source-set.md) (v0.298, [#4154](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4154)) replaced the list with the source-set rule in §28.3, §28.14, §28.9 and the code-generation reference together, so a closure bound to a plain arm-local is admitted by §28.3 itself and §31.1's paragraph says so. What stays declined is stating such an admission in §31.1 alone. The syntactic pre-pass stays admissible as a **conservative implementation** of the rule (it accepts nothing the rule rejects), recorded at its site and narrowed under #3569; it is not the rule. |
| Task-only type set for the fresh-store lint | `rejected` | rationale §5.28 (*The doctrine's second and third instances*, v0.243); [proposal 0081](0081-abandonment-lints.md); spec §15.4 | —(none stated). "a task-only extension was declined because it would have left the middle band (`conns.Add(new Connection("a"))` — non-trivial, non-task) as the *only* fresh store shape with no diagnostic, an inverted asymmetry stranger than the one being fixed." The lint's type set is every non-trivial-dispose class. |
| `ToArray()` on a span over class-typed or borrow-bearing elements | `rejected` | stdlib §4.14 (*No materialization on this family*, v0.277; the member existed v0.222–v0.276, #1810); spec §9.7 | —(none stated), and **moot since v0.277**: the whole member was deleted, so this narrower carve-out no longer scopes anything live. Its grounds stand as the record any future materializer must answer — at class-typed `T` the buffer owns the class slots and materializing them would require duplicating ownership or implicit cloning, both foreclosed (consume with `move span[i]`); at borrow-bearing value records §9.7's copy-into-collection check is a store-site rule and the store site inside such a method is invisible to the caller-side checker. A materializer for either family is now the row above's *"stdlib pass on its own merits"*. |
| Truthiness in conditions | `rejected` | rationale §17.1 | —(none stated). |
| Type tests and bindings as pattern-alternative members (`a \| b`) | `rejected` | rationale §3.8 (*Pattern alternatives are spelled `\|`*) | —(none stated). "A type member stays a compile error; grouped type tests are separate arms" — constant-vs-type alternatives have different exhaustiveness and binding semantics, reasoning unchanged by the v0.167 respelling. v0.183 widened the admitted members to constants, ranges, and relationals (binding-free pure membership tests); "the exclusion of types and bindings is the decision, and it stands". |
| Type-argument inference edge powers — binding the enclosing call's parameters through a nested lambda's own parameter positions (phase-2 re-entry), a collection-literal lambda body as a return-binding source or as a deferred body typed after a later argument or phase 3, and choosing among non-unique interface instantiations (the v0.292 rulings, [#1695](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/1695)) | `rejected` | spec §14.5 (phase 1's non-unique clause; phase 2's no-return-binding sentences); spec §22.6 (*Lambda-literal arguments*, the converting-never-exact sentence); rationale §10 (the v0.184 inference paragraph); changelog v0.292 | —(none stated) as a bar. Each is the §14.5 no-choice/no-join posture applied to one more edge (rationale §10); the literal case rests additionally on the uniform target-derived literal rule (rationale §5.16 — the *Value-element inference for collection literals* row in this section) and on phase 2 being a single left-to-right pass. Reopen with the evidence that row demands, plus a stated agreement rule for any binding a nested lambda would introduce, or a stated second pass with its own failure rule for the deferred-literal case. |
| Type-argument inference powers — bound-set fixpoints, best-common-type, constraint-driven inference, and partial explicit lists | `rejected` | rationale §10 (*Generics* — the v0.184 inference paragraph); spec §14.5 | Recorded as **liftable**, not barred: "Each declined power … is liftable later without rejecting a program this rule set accepts." The reasons are stated per power: bindings must agree exactly and the compiler computes no common supertype ("`Pick(1, 2L)` is an error, not `long`"), the §27.3/§20 no-LUB posture applied to inference, "so a call's meaning never shifts because a distant hierarchy edit changed a join"; explicit lists are all-or-nothing; constraints are checked after inference rather than feeding it. |
| Typed shift counts (Zig-style `u5`/`u6` widths) | `rejected` | rationale §14.7; [proposal 0079](0079-shift-count-domain.md) | —(none stated). "needs `u5`/`u6` widths the type system should not grow for one operator." |
| Type-level exclusion list for auto-equality (`equality(Lo, Hi)`) | `rejected` | rationale §7.5; [proposal 0084](0084-equality-skip.md) | —(none stated). The kept-synchronized-list failure mode; `equality skip` is per-member instead. |
| Unions and bitfields at the FFI boundary | `rejected` | spec §32.13 item 6; rationale §16.5 | —(none stated). "no structural spelling survives §3.6’s computed-never-declared discipline; bitfield layout is ABI-quirky." Two workarounds are recorded in place. |
| User constructors, instance fields, and storage-backed properties on a `choice` | `rejected` | spec §35.2 (*Members* — "by design") | —(none stated). A user constructor "would have to pick a case, which the qualified case form already spells"; independent instance storage alongside the payload union "would make every value part-product-part-sum, destroying the one-active-case invariant that exhaustiveness and disposal both lean on". |
| Value-element inference for collection literals (`var x := [1, 2, 3];`) | `rejected` | rationale §5.16 (2026-08 elegance review) | —(none stated). Three grounds; the first is that the uniform target-derived literal-tree rule is load-bearing for ownership. |
| Value-position conditional alternatives — `if c then a else b end`, C's `c ? a : b`, and the bare unparenthesized `if c then a else b` | `rejected` | rationale §3.3 (*Alternatives declined*); [proposal 0057 §6](0057-paren-delimited-expression-forms.md) | —(none stated) for these three; the *postfix* form's retirement is its own row and carries the standing-discipline bar. `if … end` fell to end-noise inside nested expressions and "the adopted form is its repair"; `c ? a : b` is "two more sigils in a language that spells `and`/`or`/`not` as words"; the bare form has a statement-position prefix collision and dangling-`else` under nesting. |
| Value-position conditional — the three declined rival spellings (`if c then a else b end`, `c ? a : b`, the bare unparenthesized form) | `rejected` | rationale §3.3 (*The value-position conditional…*, v0.208; proposal 0057) | —(none stated). "Alternatives declined": `if c then a else b end` for "`end`-noise inside nested expressions" (the adopted paren-delimited atom "is its repair"); `c ? a : b` as "two more sigils in a language that spells `and`/`or`/`not` as words"; and the bare unparenthesized form for "statement-position prefix collision with the statement form, dangling-`else` under nesting". The **postfix** form is a separate record — it shipped and was retired, and already has its own `retired` row. |
| Warning-not-error for the un-awaited task drop (admitting `_ := task;` and warning on every un-awaited drop — S2-03 option (a)) | `rejected` | changelog v0.288 (L8); rationale §5.23, §5.28 | —(none stated). Would attach a permanent, unsuppressible warning to §30.10's own legal `using var t := call();` idiom — rationale §5.28 records that no per-site suppression exists for the fresh-store family, deliberately. The anonymous loophole is closed by rejection instead. |
| Widening `enum` to carry payloads | `rejected` | rationale §9.6 | —(none stated). Every joint of the integer-backed enum contract would need an "unless it has payloads" fork. Two keywords, two clean contracts. |
| Widening §22.12's `const` type requirement to enum types (the reading rule 3's wording invited before v0.291) | `rejected` | changelog v0.291 (the enum-`const` clarification block); spec §22.12 rule 3 and the `const` paragraph after rule 5 | A use an enum-typed `static readonly` does not already serve warning-free, together with a §17.14 that folds enum operands — which its literal-rooted sublanguage deliberately does not. |
| Widening the pure-body `for` elision to generic-parameter and interface-typed sources | `rejected` | rationale §9.7 (*The pure-body `for` widening*, v0.244); [proposal 0085](0085-pure-for-elision.md); spec §22.15 rule 5 | —(none stated). "generic type parameters stay rejected even when every instantiation is a concrete sequence type, because §22.15's checking discipline is single-pass over bodies by design … admitting loops per-instantiation would make body-statement admissibility substitution-dependent, a checker-architecture change for one loop form"; "Interface-typed sources stay rejected on the elision's own condition ('see the type concretely' is false by construction)." `IReadOnlySpan<TElement>` is separately deferred — see its `deferred` row. |
| Write through a value-narrowed binding at a boxed location — the box-write and dropped-write readings | `rejected` | rationale §11.6 (v0.254, spec defect [#3117](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/3117)); spec §12.7 | —(none stated). Reading (1) *legal, writes the box* is "locally self-consistent … and globally unsound": `Object` is a reference type, so the box is shared and the write is "aliased mutation of a value, the one thing value-copy semantics exists to exclude"; it also breaks the runtime-managed kind's own premise. Reading (3) *legal, reflected nowhere* "was never live: a silently dropped write". The adopted rule keys on where the store lands, so `o += 1` (whose §6.6 expansion targets the declared location) stays legal. |

### `deferred`

| Topic | Verdict class | Authoritative record | Reopening bar |
|---|---|---|---|
| Type arguments on an enclosing segment in a type position (`Tree<int>.Node<int>`), identity-inert, fixing only the segment's arity | `deferred` | spec §11.6, A.5 (v0.316); rationale §9.10 (v0.316 paragraph); changelog v0.316 ([#5386](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5386)) | A grammar change whose arguments take no part in identity, inviting the reading that `Tree<int>.Node<int>` and `Tree<string>.Node<int>` differ; a top-level declaration gives callers a nameable type today. Reopening bar: a recorded need a top-level declaration cannot meet, filed as a proposal. |
| A callee-carried "stores into `ref`/`out` slot" fact narrowing §28.3's call-site `ref`/`out` pool | `deferred` | spec §28.3 *Borrow lifetime rules* (v0.309); changelog v0.309 ([#4819](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/4819)); cf. proposal 0132 | The v0.309 rule reads only the signature and assumes every pooled borrow may reach every borrow-carrying `ref`/`out` location. Reopening bar: a signature- or interface-carried per-parameter storage fact (0132's shape) that a caller may rely on, with the over-rejection it removes measured. |
| A caller-side warning when a fresh temporary reaches a plain parameter that the callee's body stores (`holder.Hold(new Item())`), from a body-derived per-parameter storage fact | `deferred` | rationale §5.24 (v0.316 addendum); [proposal 0132 §7](0132-derived-retention-study.md); changelog v0.316 ([#5374](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5374)) | The fact would be computed from the callee's body and carried in its interface, so a body edit changes another module's diagnostics; it is a may-fact, unavailable at `virtual`, interface and function-value calls; `scoped` or `owned` already makes the bound static. Reopening bar: proposal 0132 §7's, in particular its item (3), a ruling that a body-derived fact may be interface-carried for diagnostics only; this row is the full-expression-boundary instance of that study's fact, widened to any field or borrowing-collection store. Proposal 0133's Option 2b (v0.320, [#5375](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5375): a member inadmissible at a function-type substitution when its body stores a `T`-typed parameter) is a second dependent of the same deferred fact. |
| Exempting the return-slot arm of a set-erasing position where the member's own declared return is mint-announcing (`xs.Select(lambda(x) => lambda() => svc.Run(x))` at a function-type `U`) | `deferred` | spec §28.3 (*Borrow lifetime rules*, the closure bullet's set-erasing positions) (v0.320); rationale §4.2 (*Function values at set-erasing positions*); [proposal 0133](0133-set-erasing-positions.md) §8 item 4, §10; changelog v0.320 ([#5375](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5375)) | Sound, since frame residence carries the argument's set and nothing erases it, but it splits one uniform rule for a lazy selector whose closures live only in the frame. Reopening bar: a corpus program that must produce capture-bearing closures through a lazy selector. |

| Implicit type-parameter binding through a function type's parameter and return slots in an `extension` head (`extension Array<(T) -> T>`) | `deferred` | spec §26.4 (v0.303); changelog v0.303 | §26.4's implicit binding is the language's only one and is stated exactly over a constructed generic type's argument positions; a function type's slots are not argument positions. Deferred on the same footing as §26.4's `T?` head: reopenable when a need is recorded, and the record must say how a bare identifier nested in a function slot interacts with §27.1's announcement-bearing return slots (§28.3's mint-announcing rule reads a declared slot, which a freshly-bound parameter is not). |

The **instrumented** mechanism now has a bar (0107 §3): a clause-admissibility rule that closes the frame class without minting a second per-body effect fact, **together with** a constructor-interval closure naming and closing all three escape routes — not a demonstration that the syntax reads well. The fabrication half landed separately at v0.268 as spec §9.9 (constructor-only records); §22.16's deferral is untouched by it. |

The interactions are real and are now stated as interactions, not rules. |

Any design must also answer the ownership question (an early return is a K3.2 consume of the propagated payload) and be weighed against ownership-polymorphic function parameters, which "may relieve the same pain without new control flow". Proposal 0076 §7 records that its case-study evidence is the gate's *declined* class and is not offered against it. |

Grouped one-record-one-row, as the §14.6 and *Checked enum conversion* rows are. |

The function-typed form's capture would be a `scoped` field whose never-read-out and quiescence obligations §28.14 states over a declared field; liftable by a revision that states the synthesized `scoped` capture's rules and the class's inherited quiescence obligation (0127 §2.1). |

Aliases are the borrow-liveness question rationale §5.1 declines, so a widening must say why the same-binding predicate's locality survives; the indexer-set widening needs only the shared operation vocabulary reopened; the keyed leg needs the same-key-expression precision stated over key equality (§8.3) with the no-intervening-operation condition and its own dictionary corpus pair; the general lint needs a provenance table over every §28.3 producer. |

Without the second half the measurement decides nothing: the form would make one member classify differently at a direct call than at a dispatched one, and would consume a carried per-body fact (§2.8.2 — `#sec-artifact-interface`; proposal 0105 §1) for no change in any program's meaning. |

"User classes cannot be cross-domain publication points in v1" — a user `Subscribe` is retention-incapable under the call-bounded regime; the lift path is spec §28.14’s deferred unbounded tier, so this row reopens only when the next row's bar is met **and** a user-authored publication point is shown that `SharedEventSource<T>` (stdlib §6.5) cannot stand in for. |
| Unbounded retention tier for function-typed parameters (the missing third tier beside call-bounded and `scoped`) | `deferred` | spec §28.14 (*Known v1 limitation*, pointing here since v0.292); [#1427](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/1427) (closed 2026-09-05, declined with reasons) | **The bar:** a corpus program — a stdlib surface or a real program in the tree — that must return a **composed function value** capturing received callbacks, where no bounding object exists to make it a `scoped` store (no `using var` local, no `this`-owned registration site, and no deferred-activation frame — the expressible neighbours §28.14 names), **together with** a stated rule for how `unbounded` participates in §27.1's function-type identity and in §28.14's method-reference formation bar. Measured 2026-09-05: the only `Compose` in the tree is the negative test `tests/ownership/call_bounded_2814_test.cpp:283`, which asserts the rejection; no stdlib member composes callbacks into a returned function value; the one dependent want is the row above. The recorded cost and shape stand: "combinators that compose received callbacks into a returned **function value** (`Compose(f, g)` returning `lambda(x) => g(f(x))`) are not expressible over call-bounded parameters — the returned closure would capture them beyond the call, and no bounding frame exists"; the marker would admit "only capture-free or transitively unbounded arguments, which the callee may then retain freely". A second instance (v0.320, proposal 0133, [#5375](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/5375)): a selector `Pick<T>(a: T, b: T): T` over capture-bearing closures is rejected at its set-erasing parameters, and a function-typed `Pick(a: () -> int, b: () -> int): () -> int` returns a call-bounded parameter; the expression form `(if c then f else g)` is the remedy. |

NFKC/NFKD: "the compatibility tables double the data size for use cases that are niche in Bezel's target domain"; the comparer/collation surface is `System.Globalization` territory; span normalization is "additive, deferred". |

Recorded §18-style, not §17: what stands in the way is unbuilt machinery. The decline reaches **one of three poles**: value-carried units need no language change and ship in their single-dimension case as `TimeSpan` (stdlib §2.5). |

The category exists only to support owning inline arrays and owning-classified choices; the one anticipated extension that did arrive (owning choices, v0.200) came from the language, not from a user declaration. Distinct from the *Move-only value types in `OwningBuffer<T>` / `Span<T>` / `ReadOnlySpan<T>`* row, which is about admitting the existing category at those element positions. |
