A compile error at the declaration today, because "variance is defined by instance-reference assignability, [and] the position rules have no recorded reading for static signatures". |

Not the same diagnostic: a different member set, a different attachment site, and it fires on the whole materialize-then-loop idiom — including `for var x in Load(p).ToArray() do … end`, a silent certain fault with no accessor in sight. Ruling it in also requires re-weighing stdlib §5's `GroupBy` advice, which currently *recommends* the shape such a warning would fire on. |

The assignment target is a written declared type at which §14.5 already completes, so that half is principled; argument position is not — `f(new())` against `f(A)`/`f(B)` has no target before candidate selection (proposal 0122 §3 L17's refutation). Not taken at v0.296: a widening changes what compiles and is a design change. |

The motivating cost is real and stated: under v0.276's ruling a record with an `IntPtr`/`UIntPtr`/`Ptr<T>` component is not auto-hashable and cannot use `=` or key a hash container. Routed rather than ridden on the ruling that exposed it. |

### `declined-at-proposal`

| Topic | Verdict class | Authoritative record | Reopening bar |
|---|---|---|---|
| A borrowing `SlotMap<T>` twin of `OwningSlotMap<T>` | `declined-at-proposal` | [proposal 0130 §2.3 item 10, §4 item 3](0130-owning-slot-map.md) (graduated v0.307) | —(none stated). Handles exist to replace stored borrows; a borrowing form would reintroduce the per-slot store residual the type retires. |
| An always-on trust-admission warning (the report mode in the default build) | `declined-at-proposal` | [proposal 0131 §4 item 2](0131-report-trust-admissions.md) (graduated v0.307); rationale §5.31 | A measured count of field-held admissions under the mode showing the default-build cost is small — that is, the `scoped` spelling has become the norm. |
| A user-writable per-member `where` naming an enclosing type parameter (conditional members, option A) | `declined-at-proposal` | [proposal 0116 §5.1, §9 R1](0116-conditional-members.md); spec §14.2 rule 1, Appendix C.1 (v0.277) | **Suspended, not withdrawn.** The stated bar — real Bezel code where §26.4's constrained extension is reached for and fails for a reason *other* than §26.5 rule 1's name collision — presumes a mechanism that does not currently function: [#3336](https://github.com/s8lap/bezel-lang/issues/3336) establishes that a block `where` clause does not filter extension applicability at all. The bar can be neither met nor missed until that lands. Proposal 0116 §5.5 argues it is aimed at the wrong thing — Rust, Swift and C++20 all chose this option, and Rust chose it *because* separate impl blocks collide on the name — and recommends it be re-drawn rather than defended as written. |
| Abandonment-lint alternatives — rejection instead of a lint, a task-family-only lint A, a type-system fix for lint B, and runtime-only checked-mode faulting | `declined-at-proposal` | [proposal 0081 §4](0081-abandonment-lints.md); spec §29.11, §28.3; rationale §5.28 | —(none stated). Rejection "cannot be scoped by signature" for A (`coll.Add(new X())` and `sink.Consume(new X())` are indistinguishable plain-parameter calls) and would be "trivially launderable through one helper" for B; a task-only lint A "would leave precisely the middle band as the only fresh store shape with no diagnostic — an inverted asymmetry stranger than today's"; the type-system fix "changes stdlib §5's v0.235-scoped contract … to fix a subset the lint can name precisely"; runtime-only "fires at the *use*, far from the defect". |
| Admit-but-don’t-rename (the §22.6 owned-overload lift without the stdlib merge) | `declined-at-proposal` | [proposal 0091 §7](0091-owned-overload-lift.md) alternative 2 | —(none stated). "Viable as staging; declined as an end state"; offered as that proposal’s Q9 fallback. |
| Anonymous types (C#-style ad-hoc object shapes) | `declined-at-proposal` | **D-0002**, in the D-0001…D-0008 register below | "tuples being reopened first (the recorded deferral names evidence-from-real-code as the bar) — named tuples would then be re-weighed against anonymous types, and would still win." The accepted remedy (method-local `record`/`enum`) graduated at v0.225 to rationale §9.10. |
| Arm-position transfer alternatives — implicit consume in arms, and a whole-expression marker | `declined-at-proposal` | [proposal 0056 §6 items 2–3](0056-arm-position-transfer-marker.md) | —(none stated). Implicit consume "violates K3.13's no-silent-consume. Non-starter." The whole-expression marker (`move (a if c else b)`) "marks an expression rather than a binding; *what* is consumed is no longer named at the consume site; composes wrongly with mixed arms". The `??`-operand and field-path items of the same register are the axiom-A7 `deferred` row. |
| BigInteger, now | `declined-at-proposal` | [stdlib gap review](stdlib-gap-review-2026-07.md) §"Declined-with-reasons" item 2 | "Ride the crypto milestone; record the dependency so crypto is not built on intrinsics." |
| Bit-tier advance shapes — fused `out`, mediated heap cursor, named-record fused return | `declined-at-proposal` | stdlib §4.14 (*Bit-granular accessors* — no offset parameter); changelog v0.264; [proposal 0109 §2 D2, §6 Q3](0109-bit-reader-writer.md) | —(none stated). Slice-and-walk was ruled on a language fact, not taste: Bezel has no destructuring variable binding (spec §19.3; rationale §5.17), so the fused named-record return costs three statements per read against slice-and-walk's two. The `out`-fused form inverts stdlib §1's `out`-is-the-failure-channel convention; the mediated heap cursor is **legal** under spec §9.4's mediating-hop rule and is declined on design grounds (an allocation per view, a cursor shared by every copy of a value type, a record whose value is not its state). |

The typed tier already exists next door: stdlib §4.14's `Read{Int,UInt}{16,32,64}{Little,Big}Endian` serve every byte-aligned width, and the bit tier exists for widths that have no natural type. A width-selected tier would quadruple the member count to serve the case already served; the narrowing cast is total (spec §5.2). |

The decisive ground is **dispatch** — §22.15 rules 1 and 3 propagate a `pure` override down the chain, so marking it would newly *reject* every derived exception type's existing override, shipped corpus code included. Restricting only the arm side "is incoherent: the throw side is the source of the artifacts". |

Unit-returning siblings "double the API surface"; blanket-silencing trivial-dispose discards "would also silence genuinely-forgotten computed values (`s.Trim();`)". The landed rule is narrower than the draft in two ways recorded at graduation: the result's static type must **equal** the receiver's (else same-source queries like `FirstOrNull()` would be silenced), and the exemption covers method-call results only, not accessor reads — base-typed fluent returns stay conservatively warned. |
| Function-type identity under owned-erasure — candidate (a), one uniform indirect convention language-wide | `declined-at-proposal` | [proposal 0104 §1.2, §1.5](0104-witness-abi-open-questions.md) | "If (b′) fails adversarial review, (a) is the sound fallback and its tax must then be re-priced, not assumed away." |
| Function-type identity under owned-erasure — candidate (b), value-wrapping reabstraction thunks | `declined-at-proposal` | [proposal 0104 §1.3, §1.5](0104-witness-abi-open-questions.md) | —(none stated). "equality-transparency engineering plus allocation-bearing fatal sites, degenerating into (a)." |

| HTTP server — a `Before`/`After` hook pair in place of one `Around` hook | `declined-at-proposal` | [proposal 0002 §7 Q7](0002-http-server.md) (re-draft 2026-09-06) | —(none stated). `Before` cannot log the response status and `After` cannot short-circuit; the pair is two hooks plus an ordering rule between them. One `Around(req, next)` on the table — `next` call-bounded and awaited to settlement (spec §28.14 *Call-bounded × deferred activation*) — covers auth, logging, timing and exception conversion, with nesting as the only order. |
| HTTP server — a `Body<T>` marker record on typed handlers (`Create(body: Body<NewTodo>)`) | `declined-at-proposal` | [proposal 0002 §7 Q4](0002-http-server.md) (typed handlers 2026-09-09) | Evidence of a capability/route assignment mismatch reaching a *serving* table — structurally impossible while every table is built in the service constructor before the first accept, so the count rule's `ArgumentException` (band 1) fires at construction or in the first `Dispatch` test. The marker would make the body's role compile-time exact at the price of a wrapper on every body handler and every direct test call (`body.Value.Title`, `api.Create(new Body<NewTodo>(…))`), defeating the ordinary-method bar the typed design exists for. |
| HTTP server — a fourth handler parameter (`req` + two captures + a body at one slot list) | `declined-at-proposal` | [proposal 0002 §7 Q3, §3](0002-http-server.md) (typed handlers 2026-09-09) | —(none stated). The family is thirteen members per verb (the classic shape plus three result arms × four arities); each further arity adds three per verb for a shape an `HttpRequest` slot spells in one line (`req.Param<T>`). Three covers two captures plus a body, or the request plus a capture plus a body. |
| HTTP server — a synchronous handler arm (`(A) -> R` / `(HttpRequest) -> owned HttpResponse` beside the `Task` forms) | `declined-at-proposal` | [proposal 0002 §7 Q3](0002-http-server.md) (re-draft 2026-09-06; typed handlers 2026-09-09) | Originally cost-only (it would double a six-member surface). Since the typed family it is **structural**: spec §14.5 phase 1 binds a bare `R` to "the argument's static type", so an `async` reference `(int) -> Task<Todo>` binds `R := Task<Todo>` against a sync `(A) -> R` slot exactly as it binds `R := Todo` against `(A) -> Task<R>` — two exact generic candidates, ambiguous under §22.6. A sync arm and a typed async arm cannot share a name. Reopening needs a spelling that keeps the bare-`R` slot out of the async reference's reach (a distinct method name), and evidence that `async lambda() => …` is a real burden. |
| HTTP server — name-based binding of captures and query parameters (`id` ↔ `{id}`, `q` ↔ `?q=`) | `declined-at-proposal` | [proposal 0002 §7 Q4](0002-http-server.md) (typed handlers 2026-09-09); spec §27.1 (v0.295) | —(none stated). Function-type parameter names "are documentation only; they do not affect type identity" (§27.1), and a formed method reference may drop or relabel them: a binding keyed on a name would read what the type system has erased, and would need a name table no rule of the language keeps (rationale §5.18). Captures bind by type and position; query parameters — named and optional by nature — stay `req.Query<T>(name): T?` on an `HttpRequest` slot. |
| HTTP server — route-pattern typing (`{id:int}` in the route string) | `declined-at-proposal` | [proposal 0002 §7 Q4](0002-http-server.md) (re-draft 2026-09-06) | —(none stated). The table would have to *hold* typed values without knowing `T` — a typed bag is reflection-shaped (rationale §5.18). Access-time `req.Param<T>(name)` is ordinary spec §11.8 constraint dispatch over `IParsable<T>` (stdlib §2.19) with zero reflection, and a parse failure is one `HttpResponseException(400)` the dispatcher converts (stdlib §1 band 4). Since the typed-handler pass (2026-09-09) the type is in the handler's signature — `GetById(id: int)` — so a route-string type would be a second, redundant declaration of it. |
| HTTP server — the `HttpApp` class (a per-worker route table and a static lifecycle under one name; the 2026-07-24 design's centre) | `declined-at-proposal` | [proposal 0002 §7 Q10, §8](0002-http-server.md) (re-draft 2026-09-06) | —(none stated). Retired at the redraft on the v0.294 trace: its `Get(route, handler)` took a *plain* (call-bounded) function parameter and stored it, which spec §28.14 forbids outright, and its route builder received the table as a *parameter* — a bounding object that admits capture-free callbacks only — so the §2.1 showcase (`app.Get("/todos/{id}", api.GetById)`) did not compile. Conflating the two roles is also what let 0021 §4.3 write `new HttpApp()` and an instance `Listen`. Split into `RouteTable` (owned by each service; registration bounds by `this`, form 4) and the static `HttpServer`. |

The one-liner recompiles; the hot loop uses `new Regex` once; "the cost is legible either way". |

A fix-up registry "would be exactly the *hidden per-type runtime* shape whose retraction rationale §5.28 records — declined on recorded temperament, not just cost". The invariant fences §28.9's latitude away from moving or compacting allocators for the runtime-managed types: "if one is ever wanted, this invariant is the recorded decision to reopen." Mechanical regeneration keeps "two normative statements of one truth". |

An earlier draft of 0116 would have registered `ReadOnlySpan<T>.ToArray()` — which the compiler *rejects* — and §5's hashable operators, which need no privilege at all: two fictions in a normative table, with a growth discipline attached. A row must name a member the compiler admits, or say on its face that it does not (the `DangerousPointer()` row's shape). This is the asserting half of what `spec-change-process.md` §5 item 3b forbids. |

### `non-goal`

| Topic | Verdict class | Authoritative record | Reopening bar |
|---|---|---|---|
| ABI stability across recompilation | `non-goal` | spec §2.8.5 | "Revisiting one requires new evidence — real programs hitting real walls — against the stated reason." The model is recompile-the-world; [0103 §7 item 4](0103-shared-value-generics-study.md) notes this bullet is unmeetable while there are no external users. |
| Conditional compilation (`#if`, feature flags) | `non-goal` | spec §2.8.5 | As above. "One source text, one meaning"; platform variance belongs in the build tool selecting *which files* compile. The gap review’s cfg/#ifdef decline is its stdlib-side twin. |
| Diagnostic identity — an error-code registry or message taxonomy | `non-goal` | spec Appendix D (*Diagnostic identity*) | "A future revision may introduce a code registry if multiple implementations materialize; until then, requiring exact messages would freeze prose this document revises freely." The spec "deliberately defines **no** error-code registry or message taxonomy"; it mandates only *when* a diagnostic is produced, and conformance claims classify outcomes only. |
| Package management, versioning, registries | `non-goal` | spec §2.8.5 | As above. "a package-manager design question for a later stage of the language’s life." |
| Re-exports (`public import`) | `non-goal` | spec §2.8.5 | As above. Forwarding would break spec §2.8.3’s naming rule "for zero expressiveness". |
| Selective imports (`import M.{X}`) | `non-goal` | spec §2.8.5 | As above. "a second place where a file’s name set is edited, for no capability qualification doesn’t already give." |
| Wildcard imports | `non-goal` | spec §2.8.5 | As above. "Nothing to add: `import M;` already makes all of `M`’s public names visible." |

"The capability had no recorded motivating use and no reporting surface"; its entire cost was rules — the no-universal-catch-all caveat, the `Object`-base carve in the multi-type binding rule, the asterisk on deliberate suppression, the hedged §31.1 parenthetical — "each a trap for the C#/Java reader who assumes a final `Exception` arm catches everything". Corpus: of ~750 `throw new` sites, none used it. Four alternatives declined with it: the status quo; requiring derivation as a **warning** ("keeps every artifact alive … the payoff is the deletions, which only a hard rule buys"); **structural admission** of any class exposing the reporting members ("Bezel has no structural typing anywhere"); and **restricting the arm side** while keeping the throw side permissive ("incoherent"). |

### `recommended — awaiting owner ruling`

> **Empty at v0.269.** The class stays in the vocabulary above because a draft proposal may again recommend a decline before one is ruled; a row sits here only while that is true, and leaves by an owner ruling that moves it into `rejected` or `deferred` with a real record. The four rows this group held at first publication were proposal 0106's recommendations, all four ruled by the owner on 2026-08-20 and landed at v0.263 — bit-syntax patterns and contract-carrying type aliases into `rejected`, forcible domain teardown into `rejected` on rationale §12.4's extended ground, and units of measure at the type-level-erased pole into `deferred` at rationale §18. Each is now indexed under its own class, pointing at its landed record rather than at the study.

First published at v0.262-era with 171 rows: 74 · 51 · 25 · 6 · 11 · 4.)* 

**The v0.269 completeness sweep (owner-directed).** This file's opening sentence claims to be the single ground-truth for where every recorded decline lives. Until this revision it was not: **203 of these 410 rows were added by a fifteen-lane sweep on 2026-08-20**, and every one records a decision taken and written down long ago that the index did not list. The gap was not random. The rationale's two **registers** (§17, §18) were swept clean when the index was built; **everything else** was not — topical rationale sections, spec per-section decision records, the **stdlib** (a home the opening paragraph did not even enumerate, though rows already cited it), and above all the **graduated proposals**, whose decline registers had ~20 of 69 headings cited. Two counting notes for anyone re-deriving these totals: one `rejected` row (*Structural / anonymous unions*) carries an escaped pipe in its topic cell, so a naive per-row regex undercounts by one; and the earlier v0.268-stamped figures of 208 and 235 were interim states of this same sweep, which is why the v0.268 **changelog** records 207 — that entry is right, and these rows postdate it.

**Two scope rules, ruled 2026-08-20 so they are not re-argued each sweep.** *(1) Status gates the record.* A decline is indexed when it is recorded in a **graduated** proposal, in an **accepted** one, or as a **delegated ruling** under the owner's standing delegation — the pre-existing *Typed modular arithmetic* row already cited an `accepted` proposal, so this is precedent rather than invention. *(2) A recommendation is not a verdict.* An under-review draft's proposed declines are **not** indexed, and the `recommended — awaiting owner ruling` class stays empty: this is a register of decisions, the class's own definition says such an item is *"not yet a verdict"*, and populating it would add rows that all change on acceptance — churn, not ground truth. "The alternative is not recommended" inside an *Open questions* list is likewise not a record.

## Part 2 — declined proposals: the register

*These records are the authority for every index row that cites them; the index never restates a reason. The register is otherwise stable: it grows by new D-numbers, and its existing text is not rewritten. One sanctioned amendment has been made to text already here — at v0.263 the three declines that had been stacked as unnumbered paragraphs under D-0004 were given their own numbers as D-0005, D-0006, and D-0007, so that each is separately citable; their reasoning is unchanged, and the repairs that split entailed (a lead sentence for D-0006, a corrected count and a restated bar for D-0005) are noted in that revision's changelog entry.*

Design directions considered for the standard library / platform and **declined with reasons**, so the question stays settled rather than perennially reopened (the same discipline as the rationale's decline records, applied to the proposals folder). Revisiting an entry requires new evidence against the stated reasons, not re-asking.

---

## D-0001 — A dependency-injection container (runtime service resolution)

**Declined 2026-07-03 (owner-confirmed).** Full reasoning in [proposal 0003 §3, item 3](0003-locked-t.md) (*"It writes down the DI answer"*); summary: *(Citation corrected 2026-08-20: this record cited §3.3, which does not exist — 0003's §3 carries no subsections. The index row had flagged the dangling pointer; the register body still carried it.)*

DI does two jobs, and Bezel keeps one while declining the other.

- **Wiring** (constructor injection) needs no framework: it is constructor parameters from a composition root, and Bezel's `owned`/borrow annotations make the dependency contract *more* expressive than any container can (`constructor(owned db: Database)` states who owns the dependency; no container expresses lifetime-of-ownership at all).
- **The container** (`AddSingleton`/`Resolve<T>()`) is declined on three grounds:
  1. **It is the same global state, hidden.** A container's singleton table *is* a process-global mutable `Dictionary<Type, object>`; every §22.12/§30.14 data-race hazard of a mutable static applies identically — ASP.NET's own "singleton services must be thread-safe" rule is §22.12 enforced by documentation instead of a compiler. A `static readonly x: Locked<T>` states the sharing, the type, and the synchronization in one visible line.
  2. **It trades compile-time for runtime.** Service-locator resolution fails at request time ("no registration for `ICache`"); a constructor parameter fails at compile time. Bezel's design consistently moves this class of error to compile time (e.g. sendability checked at route registration, proposal 0002 §4.1).
  3. **The machinery is reflection-shaped.** Runtime containers reflect over constructors; Bezel deliberately has no member-access reflection (rationale §18) — a foundational decline this would reopen.

The lifetime taxonomy maps without a framework: *singleton* → composition-root local passed down, or a §22.12 synchronized static (`Locked<T>`) when genuinely ambient; *scoped/per-request* → handler locals; *transient* → construct it.

**What would reopen this:** large real codebases demonstrating that hand-written composition roots do not scale — and the remedy would then be **compile-time generated wiring** (Dagger/source-generator style), a tooling project, never a runtime container.

---

## D-0002 — Anonymous types (C#-style ad-hoc object shapes)

**Declined 2026-07-03 (owner conversation on data projections).** Three structural grounds:

1. **They require structural typing inside a nominal system** — two same-shaped anonymous types must unify, which imports a second typing discipline for one convenience.
2. **They cannot cross method boundaries** — C#'s own recorded limitation, mitigated there only via `object`/`dynamic`, neither of which Bezel will have. A shape that cannot be returned, stored, or passed is a local-only convenience…
3. **…whose killer habitat is reflection.** Anonymous types earn their keep in ORM expression trees that read property names off the shape at runtime — machinery Bezel deliberately lacks (rationale §18). C# itself largely migrated to *named* ValueTuples once available.

**The accepted remedy** is method-local `record` (and `enum`) declarations — **graduated into the normative documents at spec v0.225** (proposal [0066](0066-method-local-type-declarations.md); language spec §21.10; the design record is now rationale §9.10, which supersedes this paragraph as the remedy's home): the shape lives next to its single use, keeping the recorded records-over-tuples stance ("a name and a documented shape is a feature") while removing the distance cost. Cross-type mapping stays explicit (constructors/factory methods; `with` for same-type variants) — automatic mapping is reflection-shaped and would be reconsidered only as compile-time generation, per D-0001's wiring posture. Truly dynamic data goes through the JSON DOM (gap review item 21): runtime-shaped values behind typed faults, quarantined at the parse boundary, never a `dynamic` type.

**What would reopen this:** tuples being reopened first (the recorded deferral names evidence-from-real-code as the bar) — named tuples would then be re-weighed against anonymous types, and would still win.

---

## Gap-review declines (2026-07)

Eight further declined-with-reasons entries — metrics API (pending #1363), BigInteger (rides crypto), blocking process waits (§30 discipline), `CurrentDirectory` setter (fights domain isolation), `String.Format` (second format convention), compile-time platform branching (language change), overwrite-on-full ring buffer (inverts ownership legibility), implicit Unicode normalization in equality (§9.6 ordinal contract) — are recorded in [the gap review](stdlib-gap-review-2026-07.md) §"Declined".

---

## D-0003 — Unifying single-statement member/lambda bodies on `do … end` (#745)

**Declined 2026-07-07 (delegated ruling, 2026-07 spec/design run; owner review pending).** The draft RFC (#745) proposed one uniform `do … end` spelling for every single-statement body position (members, lambdas, arms). Three grounds:

1. **The load-bearing seam is already closed.** The v0.170 uniform-closer revision (#1374) fixed the actual inconsistency the RFC was written against — every block now closes with `end;` or a labelled repeat of its introducer. What remains is a *spelling triad* (`=> expr` arrows, `do … end` blocks, full member bodies) whose members are each locally consistent and non-overlapping; unifying them buys no rule deletions in the grammar, only migration.
2. **The churn cost has inverted.** When the RFC was drafted the compiler was young; today ~10,500 tests, the conformance corpus, both guides, and every proposal encode the current spellings. A whole-corpus migration to retire zero rules is the definition of churn.
3. **The surviving argument depends on a declined feature.** The RFC's strongest remaining case (arm bodies as uniform blocks) reads naturally only under block-*expressions*, which rationale §5.13 declines on recorded grounds; without them the unified form is strictly more ceremony in the common case.

**What would reopen this:** block expressions being reopened first (their own register entry governs), or field evidence that the triad measurably confuses users — not re-asking.

---

## D-0004 — `Option<T>` in the standard library

**Declined 2026-07-19 (0041 graduation, v0.200).** `T?` is Bezel's option type: it has language-level syntax (`?.`, `??`, narrowing, the §19.6 nullable clause), stdlib-wide integration (the Try shapes, `FirstOrNull`), and a design lineage the rationale defends throughout. Shipping `Option<T>` beside the `choice` kind would create two spellings of absence, two conversion questions at every API boundary, and an eternal style war. A choice with one meaningful payload and one empty case *is* what `T?` already provides. Revisiting requires evidence that `T?` structurally cannot serve where `Option<T>` could — not preference.

---

## D-0005 — A value-form try expression (`(try E catch …)` / `(try E else fallback)`)

**Declined 2026-07-21 (expression-forms review, post-v0.214).** The first of two paren-atom candidates weighed together in that review — the second is D-0006 — each by the admission test recorded in proposal 0061: **a value-form try expression** (`(try E catch … )` / `(try E else fallback)`) — fails test 3: it makes exception-based *expected* failure ergonomic, precisely the path Guide 2 §6 (v0.214-era numbering; the table is §7 since the 2026-07-30 restructure) routes to `T?`/Try/`Result`; the doctrine would be undermined by its own syntax, and the borrow-scoped catch model (§31) offers no owned exception value to carry anyway.

Revisiting requires new evidence against these reasons.

---

## D-0006 — Comprehension expressions (`(for x in xs where p => f(x))`)

**Declined 2026-07-21 (expression-forms review, post-v0.214).** The second of the two paren-atom candidates weighed in that review — the first is D-0005 — by the same proposal 0061 admission test. **Comprehensions** (`(for x in xs where p => f(x))`) — fail test 1: pure sugar over the lazy-query operators (`xs.Where(p)…`, affine per §29.11), whose ownership story is already settled; also a loop expression, which the owner separately declined absent unique capability. Revisiting either requires new evidence against these reasons.

---

## D-0007 — Delegation-by-forwarding sugar (Go embedding / Kotlin `by`)

**Declined 2026-07-21 (writer-seat sweep).** **Delegation-by-forwarding sugar** (Go embedding / Kotlin `by`): implicit method promotion hides the forwarding call and blurs receiver identity — squarely against announced-flow/no-hidden-magic — and composition-by-explicit-forwarding is fully expressible today; the absence was boilerplate, not capability. Revisiting requires evidence that forwarding boilerplate is a measured, recurring burden the one-line members do not adequately express.

---

## D-0008 — Newtype deriving (Haskell GND, Nim `distinct` borrowing, OCaml-style derived conformance)

**Declined 2026-08-20 (v0.263, recorded from proposal 0106 §5.5).** A mechanism by which a one-field wrapper automatically obtains the wrapped type's interface conformances and members — Haskell's `deriving newtype`/GeneralizedNewtypeDeriving, Nim's `distinct` with `borrow`, and their relatives — so that a `Port` wrapping an `int` gets `int`'s comparison, formatting, or numeric surface without restating it.

Recorded explicitly rather than reached by adjacency to D-0007. **The adjacency is real but partial, and that is why this entry exists.** Both forms are implicit method promotion at the one-field case, so D-0007's ground carries: promotion hides the forwarding call and blurs receiver identity, against announced-flow/no-hidden-magic. But the mechanisms differ in a way a reader must not have to reconstruct — Kotlin `by` forwards to a *stored delegate*, an object the wrapper holds, while newtype deriving derives a *conformance* by representational coercion, on the ground that the wrapper and the wrapped type have the same representation. The extension of D-0007's ground to that case: a conformance obtained by representational coercion is a claim that the wrapper is interchangeable with what it wraps at every member of the derived interface — which is exactly the claim a distinct wrapper is declared in order to deny. Where the wrapper genuinely wants the whole surface, an alias already gives it (spec §27.8, and the constrained-alias variant is declined at rationale §17.12); where it does not, deriving the whole surface is the wrong default, and the members it actually wants are one line each.

The boundary Bezel draws around automatic derivation is drawn, not absent: equality and hashing are auto-derived for records with a per-member opt-out (spec §9.2, the `equality skip` clause), and serialization is derived by conformance (spec §9.8) — both cases where the derived behavior is a function of the type's own structure rather than a claim of interchangeability with another type.

**What would reopen this:** the same bar D-0007 states — evidence that forwarding boilerplate is a measured, recurring burden the one-line members do not adequately express — together with a design that says which conformances derive and which do not, since the failure mode is deriving the ones that assert interchangeability.

---

## D-0009 — Built-in collection-literal conformance without a callable witness; and the `DECLINED.md:30` reopening

**Declined 2026-09-05 (v0.294, recorded from proposal 0125 §2.3/§2.10).** The ten built-in sequence collections conform to the collection-literal target pair (stdlib §2.19) by nominal declaration, and the conformance rule — spec §11.8: *"A conforming type declares `class Meters: INumber<Meters>` and supplies the static members with exact signatures (the §11.2 matching rule, applied to statics)"*; *"a runtime type conforms only by declaring the full member set, statics included, so there is no partial-conformance state to probe"* — has no form in which a type conforms without a callable static witness. Two alternatives were priced and declined: a **compiler-provided** conformance (§12.7 clause 5 scopes that device to primitives; extending it to ten classes is a new implicit-conformance case), and a **witness-less** conformance (a new conformance kind for ten types, in the same family). The consequence is that `Array<T>.FromCollectionLiteral(elements: ReadOnlyArraySpan<T>): owned Array<T>` — the value the `ToArray()` row at the top of the index declined on *"surface is debt"* and *while-we're-at-it* grounds — is supplied at the witness spelling. That row is amended rather than deleted: the new evidence against its stated reasons is that the member is a **structural consequence of the promotion the owner directed** (#3247, #1506), not a convenience riding a fix; its `ToArray()` spelling stays declined as a dual. Revisiting the witness requirement takes a §11.8 design change with its own proposal.
